Research done, design written, and every mockup page open in your browser: 38 phone screens in 8 sections. Nothing committed and no app code touched. The screens are an HTML file; I rendered them headless and fixed three layout bugs I found (text missing on coloured pills, a clipped streak row, a wrapped cookie grid).

**Files**
- Mockups: `docs/superpowers/specs/mockups/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework.html`
- Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-design.md`

**What research said**
- Kids 10–15 have little patience and read literally, and they drop anything that looks "for babies".
- Children read bar charts and picture charts best, and double line charts worst. That's why the current money graph confuses them.
- A cartoon helper that shows feelings helps motivation and learning a little but measurably. Short speech bubbles beat long text.
- Streaks help kids come back (Duolingo saw 2.4× more next-day returns), but only if missing a day is forgiven.
- Random rewards and guilt messages harm children, and the UK Children's Code and India's DPDP Act restrict nudging minors.

**What changes (mockups numbered P1… parent onboarding, C1… child onboarding)**
- **Mascot:** Jarso, the brand jar, gets eyes, arms and six moods (happy, cheer, wow, think, wave, sleepy). It's never sad. Gold fills it as the child's Save grows, and kids can change its lid colour and add a cap or crown.
- **Home:** about 25 words, down from 120–180. Jarso with one line, a Spend tile, a Save tile, the goal ring, and today's mission.
- **Actions:** one gold **+** button opens "What happened?" with five picture tiles: *I bought something / I got money / Save some / Use savings / Ask for extra*. "Move my money" becomes "My asks".
- **Money flows:** each one is a big keypad, then a picture grid, then a celebration screen instead of a toast. A request waiting for the parent shows as a 3-step tracker.
- **Graph:** the line graph is gone. Each jar gets its own page with 6 weekly bars, the amount written on each bar, and one sentence.
- **Learn:** missions sit on a map path. Right answers get a green "Nailed it!", wrong ones a calm blue "Close!". A one-tap daily check-in asks "Spend anything today?".
- **Fun loop:** Sparks ⚡ points, levels that unlock Jarso looks, badges, and a streak with one free rest day a week. Sparks are never money, and there's no chance-based or paid reward.
- **Parent onboarding (P1–P7):** three swipe cards explain the app, then account, add child, pocket money, a freedom setting, and a QR card to hand to the child.
- **Child onboarding (C1–C7):** the child learns the jars by tapping and dragging practice coins (marked PRACTICE, never real), picks a goal, styles Jarso, and gets a celebration.
- **Parent app:** same look but calmer, and they approve requests with one tap on the card.

**Assumptions I made**
- I took "the graph was understandable" as a typo for *not* understandable, so the graph is replaced.
- The child side gets the full rework; the parent side gets the new look but stays plain.
- Screens say "Mum" instead of "your parent". That needs one new field the parent fills in during sign-up, which means a database change.

**Your decisions before I write the implementation plan**
1. Jarso replaces the four coach kids on the phone? I'd say yes, one character is simpler. The website keeps its coaches.
2. Splitting pocket money between Spend and Save (screen P5): in this rework, or later? It's the only change to how money rules work.
3. Parent sign-up on the phone in this rework? Today parents can only create an account on the website.

Answer those three, plus any screen you want changed, and I'll write the plan.

I've written the plan for the first wave. Nothing is implemented yet.

Everything is in this rework, so it splits into 10 smaller plans, the same way earlier mobile work was done in waves. Each one ships something working on its own. I'll write each later plan only after the one before it merges, so it builds on real code instead of guesses.

**Plan 1, Foundation:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-1-foundation.md`. It has 8 tasks, each test-first:
1. Make the test setup handle animations and sound (adds the `expo-audio` package).
2. Chunky 3D buttons in four colours (teal, gold, purple, white), with a deeper 5 dp step under each.
3. Jarso's rules in the shared core package: the six moods, lid colours, outfits and the level each unlocks at, and how full the jar looks.
4. Jarso drawn on the phone: fills with gold as Save grows, bounces when its mood changes, blinks while idle.
5. The existing "coach says" bubbles show Jarso instead of a named coach, so Learn and Notifications get the mascot straight away.
6. Three short sounds (coin, pop, cheer), made by a small script, with an on/off setting.
7. A celebration screen to replace the small pop-up messages after an action: confetti, sound, a vibration, and Jarso cheering. Two in a row show one after the other.
8. Check it on a device against the mockups, then update the design docs.

**The other nine plans, in order** (also listed in `tasks/todo.md`):
- 2: points, levels, badges and streaks stored on the server, plus the "Mum" name field
- 3: Home, the + button and the money flows
- 4: jar pages with weekly bars, and the goal
- 5: Learn map and the daily check-in
- 6: Diary, Jarso tab, Me and the bell
- 7: splitting pocket money between Spend and Save
- 8: child onboarding
- 9: parent sign-up and onboarding on the phone
- 10: parent screens restyled

**Decided without your answer:** Jarso replaces the four coach kids on the phone. The website keeps them.

Nothing is committed yet. Each task lists its commit message, but I'll only commit when you say so.

Does Plan 1 cover what you want? And how should I run it?
- **One helper per task (my pick):** a fresh helper builds each task and another checks it before the next starts. Costs more, but Plan 1 sets up the mascot, buttons and celebration that every later screen uses, so a mistake here would spread.
- **Native:** I build all 8 tasks myself, then one reviewer checks everything at the end. Cheaper and faster.

The PR is up: https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/141. It holds the first of the 10 rework plans — the base everything else builds on: the Jarso mascot, the new buttons, celebrations and sound. All checks pass, but nobody has looked at it on a phone yet, because no device was connected.

**What's in it** (9 commits, authored with your noreply email, no AI attribution):
- The design spec, the mockups and the plan.
- Jarso drawn on the phone: six moods (never sad), gold that fills with Save, outfits that unlock by level, a bounce and a blink that switch off when the phone's "reduce motion" setting is on.
- The existing coach speech bubbles now show Jarso, so Learn and Notifications get the mascot straight away.
- Chunky 3D buttons in five colours.
- Short sounds (coin, pop, cheer) with an on/off setting.
- The celebration screen: confetti, sound, vibration, and a screen-reader announcement. Nothing uses it yet; plan 3 swaps it in for the current pop-up messages.
- Docs updated.

**Checks:**
- **Passed:** lint, typecheck and every test suite (628 mobile tests), plus the web build.
- **Not run:** end-to-end tests and the database checks, because no screens, flows or migrations changed.
- **Phone check still to do (your part):** run `pnpm mobile` and compare Jarso, the buttons and a celebration against mockups H1, C6, F3 and T5.

**What the reviews changed:**
- Removed the `expo-audio` setup entry. With its defaults it would have added a microphone permission and a background playback service to a children's app; sounds play fine without it.
- The sunglasses outfit was drawn behind Jarso's eyes; it now sits in front.
- The Android back button on a celebration now does the same as its main button, so a child can't end up on a filled-in form they could submit twice.
- A broken button action on a celebration now gets logged instead of crashing the child's screens.
- The empty-Notifications bubble was invisible inside its card; it now has a contrasting colour.

**Decisions I made on your behalf:**
- **No commits until you said "raise the PR".** Work stayed uncommitted until then, as your rules require, then split into one commit per task.
- **Parallel work.** Up to 3 helpers at a time, each limited to 2 test workers to keep your machine's load down.
- **Purple button colour.** I used a slightly darker purple than the mockup so white text stays readable (meets the WCAG AA contrast standard).
- **Jarso's gold** is a slightly lighter shade than the mockup, because the mockup's gold isn't one of the fixed brand colours.
- **Sounds** play alongside the child's music instead of pausing it.
- **Left for later:** two hard-coded colours (no matching brand colour exists yet).

The session log is still uncommitted. Your rules say it goes in its own logs PR, so I haven't included it.

Next is plan 2: the server side for points, levels, badges, streaks and the "Mum" name. Want me to write it?

The Plan 2 PR is up: https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/143. Lint, typecheck, all unit tests (636 on mobile) and the web build pass. The database code has never actually run: without Docker it couldn't run here, so the PR's CI is its first real test. CI has just started.

**What it adds:** the points system behind the fun loop.
- The database awards Sparks itself when a child logs spending or money they got, saves, does a lesson step or reaches a goal milestone. The app can't add points, so they can't be gamed.
- There are daily limits, 10 levels, 23 badges, and a streak with one free rest day a week.
- It also adds the "Spend anything today?" check-in, the child's saved Jarso look, and the name a child uses for each parent ("Mum").
- On the phone, points now refresh after every money, lesson and goal action, and when a parent confirms a save.

It's 9 commits, authored with your noreply email and no AI attribution. The session log is still uncommitted for its own logs PR.

**Decisions I made on your behalf:**
1. **Lock order fix (cost: none).** The check-in answer is saved by the same database function that awards the points. The first version could deadlock against a purchase happening at the same moment.
2. **"Goal reached" badge (cost: none).** It only counts when the child actually saved toward the goal, not for a goal set below money they already had.
3. **Mission badges (cost: none).** They count different missions, so repeating one mission doesn't earn them.
4. **Known farming gap, accepted and documented (cost: a few extra Sparks for a determined child).** A child can farm goal-milestone Sparks by recording money into Save, undoing it, and starting a new goal. I accepted it because points aren't money; it's written up in `docs/domain.md`.
5. **Missing parent name (cost: none).** If no name is set, the app says "your parent".
6. **"Half a goal" badge (cost: slightly easier to earn).** It still counts if the goal was already half-saved when it was set.
7. **Late feedback on the database code (cost: problems surface late).** It was checked by careful reading only, so any problems will only show up in CI.

**Still to do:**
- **Watch CI.** If the database tests fail there, I'll send a fix to the same PR.
- **Apply the database changes to your live (hosted) Supabase.** This needs your OK (`supabase db push --linked`).
- **Regenerate the database types afterwards.** I wrote them by hand because there was no local database to generate from. Once the changes are live, I'll regenerate them and check they match.

Should I watch CI and fix anything it finds?

I opened the sign-up fix as PR #144: https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/144. It has 2 commits, authored with your noreply email and no AI attribution.

**What was wrong:** the flaky test was hiding a real app bug. The sign-up and login pages first show a placeholder copy of the form, then swap in the real form about 0.1 s after the page loads. Anything typed during that moment, by a test or a real person, was thrown away. The empty form then failed with errors like "Name is required" and the page never moved on. This hit 23 of the last 43 browser-test runs.

**The fix:**
- The placeholder form is now disabled, so nobody can type into it before the real form arrives.
- The test helper now gives up after 20 s instead of 45 s. That finishes before the test itself times out, so a future failure will report what was on screen instead of "nothing".

**How it was checked:** lint, typecheck and unit tests pass, plus a new unit test. The web build passes. The agent couldn't run the real browser tests, because they need Docker. Instead it built a small stand-in for Supabase and ran 80 sign-ups under load: 7 failed before the fix and none after. CI on the PR is the real confirmation.

**Left out on purpose:**
- **Old timing constant.** The 45-second constant in the two forms was set for a "slow server" diagnosis that turned out to be wrong; it can be revisited separately.
- **Saving traces from failed CI runs.** CI currently uploads from the wrong folder, so failed runs save nothing to debug with. Fixing that needs your decision first: those traces contain test login cookies, and this repo is public.

Want me to watch #144's CI as well?

Research task (web research, no code changes). Context: Jarso is a pocket-money learning app for children aged 10–15 (Spend jar = cash the child holds, Save jar = money kept by parent; savings goals, money diary, daily lessons/missions, AI guide). User feedback on our Expo mobile app: (1) too much text, unusable for a 10–15 year old; (2) no mascot, doesn't look like a kids app; (3) visuals need to feel like a child's app; (4) home screen hard to understand; (5) action labels like "Put some in Save", "Take some out", "Move my money", "Got money", "Ask for extra", "Spent something?" feel meaningless; (6) balance-over-time line graph not understandable.

Find evidence-backed guidance (cite sources with URLs) on:
1. UX research for children/tweens 10–15 on mobile: Nielsen Norman Group children & teens usability studies, reading level targets, text density, icons vs labels, button sizes, how tweens differ from younger kids (avoid babyish design).
2. How leading kid/teen money apps design home screens & money actions: GoHenry, Greenlight, BusyKid, Revolut <18, Step, Rooster Money, Osper, Till, Kidbank, Mydoh, plus any India-focused (FamPay, Junio) — what the first screen shows, how they visualise balances/jars/goals, how they name actions (verbs, icons).
3. Mascot design for apps (Duolingo's Duo, Headspace, Finch, Habitica, Monzo-style personality): when mascots help retention/comprehension, how they're used on home screens (state-based emotion, speech bubbles, reacting to user actions), pitfalls for 10–15 age (too childish).
4. Visualising money/savings for kids instead of line charts: filling jars, progress rings, coin stacks, bar-per-week, "you saved X more than last week" comparisons — any research on children's graph literacy (age 11–14 understanding of line graphs vs bar/pictograph).
5. Gamification patterns appropriate for this age (streaks, badges, levels, rewards) and ethical cautions for kids' finance.

Deliver a concise report (≤900 words): key findings with source links, then 10–15 concrete, actionable design principles for our redesign, then a short list of concrete UI patterns worth borrowing (which app, what pattern). Do not invent sources; if a claim lacks a source, mark it as your judgment.

timestamp: 2026-11-06T03:18:38.053Z
time: 2026-11-06 03:18
chars: 9535

## Research report: redesigning the Jarso mobile app for ages 10–15

Some of this evidence is thin. Most of the competitor and mascot material comes from reviews and press, not controlled studies, and I could not read the full NN/g reports because they are paywalled. Anything without a source is marked **[judgment]**.

### Key findings

**1. How children and teens use apps**
- NN/g's teen report says teens "often make mistakes and give up quickly" because of "insufficient reading skills", "low level of patience" and weak search strategies. It also calls them "goal-oriented". https://www.nngroup.com/reports/teenagers-on-the-web/
- Write-ups of NN/g's older teen study say teens avoid sites that look "too childlike" and would rather do things than read. These are secondary sources. https://www.youthmediareporter.org/?p=31 , https://erikrunyon.com/page4/
- NN/g on children's thinking: users up to age 12 read instructions literally. Interfaces should explain themselves and use real-world mental models. Numbers or icons not tied to a visible goal confused children. https://www.nngroup.com/articles/kids-cognition/
- NN/g on physical development: from about age 11, children can physically handle any interaction an adult can, but they "still call for simpler interfaces." The 2×2 cm minimum touch target is for young children. https://www.nngroup.com/articles/children-ux-physical-development/
- Gilutz & Nielsen found that age calibration matters. Children rejected sites they thought were meant for younger kids ("for babies"). https://www.infoworld.com/article/2225267/many-web-sites-aren-t-designed-well-for-kids.html
- Icons have no standard meaning, so they need text labels. https://www.nngroup.com/videos/icon-usability/
- The UK government writes for a reading age of 9, which is about plain words, not childish tone. https://aki-snow.github.io/govtech-marketplace-uk/www.gov.uk/guidance/content-design/writing-for-gov-uk
- Tweens find primary-colour, cartoonish, patronising designs babyish. This is from an agency guide, so it is weak evidence. https://gapsystudio.com/blog/ux-design-for-kids

**2. Competitor money apps**
- Greenlight shows Spend, Save and Give "buckets". Children see their balance, spending history and goals. Only parents move money between buckets. https://logicaldollar.com/?p=6710
- Rooster Money's child dashboard shows Spend, Save, Give and Goals pots. Allowance is split by percentage, and the app also uses a line chart of balance over time (the chart our users rejected). https://roostermoney.com/introducing-spend-save-give
- GoHenry suggests a weekly amount for each goal. Its goal pots use progress bars and celebration prompts. https://www.finextra.com/pressarticle/58177/gohenry-introduces-saving-features-for-kids , https://screenwiseapp.com/guides/money-apps-for-kids
- Revolut <18 uses goal "Pockets" (examples: phone, clothes, holiday) and keeps savings apart from spending "so you don't use it by accident". https://www.revolut.com/blog/post/revolut-under-18-the-account-built-for-teens/
- A FamPay job posting mentions a "savings streak" with rewards. https://jobs.lever.co/fampay/9fa3b26b-d8f5-4470-8eeb-ee4c2f7be336
- I found no reliable sources on Step, Mydoh, BusyKid, Junio or Osper home screens or on how they word their buttons.

**3. Mascots**
- Meta-analyses find small but real effects from on-screen characters. Emotional characters improve motivation and learning by about g≈0.26, and the effect depends on appearance, emotional cues and movement (Wang 2023). Characters that talk through on-screen text beat narration (Schroeder 2013). https://bohrium.dp.tech/paper/arxiv/5b67b49517c44aac1c865345 , https://rex.libraries.wsu.edu/esploro/outputs/journalArticle/How-Effective-are-Pedagogical-Agents-for/99900601056901842
- A 2025 meta-analysis covers school-age children (K–12), but I only saw its abstract. https://metatoc.com/papers/171620-virtual-characters-help-k-12-students-learn-and-improve-motivation-a-meta-analysis
- Duolingo drives Duo's expressions with Rive state machines. Its guilt-trip notifications work on open rates but are widely criticised. https://rive.app/blog/duolingo-s-ai-powered-video-call-brings-lily-to-life , https://platformmagazine.org/?p=29383
- Finch's pet companion is a Tamagotchi-style loop. Critics say its setup has too many options and wellbeing features sit behind a paywall. There is no peer-reviewed evidence. https://ixd.prattsi.org/2025/09/design-critique-finch-ios-app-2/

**4. Charts children can read**
- Several studies agree that children do best with pictograms and bar graphs and worst with line graphs, especially double line graphs. Pie charts are hard because they need proportional reasoning. https://funes.uniandes.edu.co/wp-content/uploads/tainacan-items/32454/1197370/Arteaga2020Chilean.pdf , https://open.lnu.se/index.php/PFS/article/download/1073/924/2671
- The goal-gradient effect: people speed up as they near a goal, and a head start helps (10 versus 15 days to finish a coffee card). https://business.columbia.edu/sites/default/files-efs/pubfiles/1200/goalgradient.pdf

**5. Gamification and ethics**
- Duolingo's own blog says learners on a 7-day streak are 2.4× more likely to come back the next day. Streak Freezes were added because a little slack motivates more than strict rules. https://blog.duolingo.com/improving-the-streak , https://blog.duolingo.com/how-duolingo-streak-builds-habit
- The UK ICO's draft Children's Code criticised reward loops and Snapchat-style streaks. The final Standard 13 is narrower and only bans nudges toward sharing data or weakening privacy. https://www.engadget.com/2019-04-15-uk-facebook-remove-like-button.html , https://edlaw.eduverse.com/docs/jurisdiction/nat/gb/childrens-code/STANDARDS.md
- A UK study of loot boxes found financial and emotional harm to children and recommends restricting chance-based rewards to adults. https://www.ncl.ac.uk/press/articles/latest/2022/11/lootboxes/
- A study of child finance apps raises concerns about gamifying household chores, monitoring children's spending, and data collection. https://d2pnhgckm19p0r.cloudfront.net/insight/content/doi/10.1108/YC-01-2024-1954/full/pdf

### Design principles for the redesign
1. **One idea per screen.** The home screen should answer three questions only: how much I have, what I'm saving for, and today's mission. Everything else goes one tap deeper. (NN/g on patience, plus judgment)
2. **Reading age of about 9, with a teen tone.** Use labels of 1–3 words and no text blocks over about 2 lines. Text should be plain but never babyish. (GOV.UK, NN/g)
3. **Show money as objects, not charts.** Use a filling jar for Save, a wallet or cash for Spend, and a progress ring with a picture of the goal item. Remove the line graph. (graph-literacy studies)
4. **For "over time", use a few bars or a sentence.** Show 4–6 weekly bars, or a line like "+₹40 more than last week". (graph-literacy studies)
5. **Every icon gets a short text label**, and each button is a concrete verb plus an object. For example, "Save ₹", "Spend", "Got money", "Ask parent". **[judgment]** Test these wording examples with children.
6. **Name actions after real events, not account mechanics.** For example, "I got paid" or "I bought something" instead of "Move my money". **[judgment]**, based on NN/g's point about familiar mental models.
7. **Show the goal as a picture with the money still needed**, e.g. "₹350 to go". Give new goals a small head start, such as counting the first deposit. (goal-gradient)
8. **Add a mascot in a supporting role.** It should react to what the child does (saves, finishes a mission, reaches a goal) with 3–5 states. Short speech bubbles beat long text (Schroeder). (meta-analyses)
9. **Make the mascot cool, not cute.** Avoid baby proportions and primary colours. Consider letting the child choose or customise it, which also covers self-expression. (NN/g age calibration, tween guides)
10. **No guilt or shame from the mascot.** Missing a day should get a neutral or encouraging response, never a crying character. (Duolingo backlash, ICO spirit)
11. **Make streaks forgiving.** Build in a free pass or freeze, and reward coming back. Base streaks on learning missions, never on spending. (Duolingo blog)
12. **No chance-based or paid rewards.** Badges and levels come only from learning and saving behaviour. (loot-box study)
13. **Celebrate milestones briefly.** A short animation is enough, without keeping children in the app longer. (ICO draft) **[judgment]**
14. **Use big touch targets**, at least about 48dp. NN/g gives no size for 11+, so this is **[judgment]**, following platform norms.
15. **Test with 10–15 year olds in two bands, 10–12 and 13–15.** NN/g: "what works for one age group will not work for another."

### UI patterns worth borrowing
- **Greenlight:** three named buckets on the child home screen, as big tiles each showing its own balance.
- **Revolut <18:** goal Pockets with a custom image, kept visibly separate from spending money.
- **GoHenry:** a suggested weekly amount for each goal, progress bars and a celebration when a goal is reached.
- **Rooster Money:** a percentage split of allowance across pots on payday. Don't copy its line chart.
- **Duolingo:** a mascot whose reactions are driven by state (Rive suits our Expo stack), a streak flame with a Streak Freeze, and a single "start today's lesson" call to action.
- **Finch:** a companion that grows as the child completes missions, without the paywall or the heavy setup.

## 1. Child navigation

**Tabs**, in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/mobile/app/(child)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` (Lucide icons only):
- `index`, "Home" → `src/features/home/home-screen.tsx`: the jars, quick actions, next step, goal and graph.
- `diary`, "Diary" → `features/history/diary-screen.tsx`: the list of purchases and money received.
- `learn`, "Learn" → `features/learn/learn-screen.tsx`: the missions roadmap. It shows `CoachSays` as a text bubble, with no drawing.
- `jarso`, "Jarso" (Sparkles icon) → `features/guide`: the AI guide chat. It only shows when the guide is turned on.
- `news`, "Notifications" (shows an unread badge) → `features/news/news-screen.tsx`: the inbox.

**Stack pages and sheets**, in `app/(child)/_layout.tsx`. Sheets open as a formSheet at 0.9 height:
- Sheets: `purchase`, `put-in`, `take-out`, `ask-extra`, `received`, `goal-sheet` (set or change the goal), `guide-sheet` (ask Jarso about something in context).
- `requests`: list of the child's requests. `me`: settings menu. `notifications`: push settings. `memories`: what Jarso remembers.
- `goal`: the goal staircase. `entry/[entryId]`: one diary line, where it can be fixed or undone.
- `learn/[missionKey]`, `learn/practice`, `learn/check-in`, `learn/topics/[topicKey]`, `learn/week`, `learn/week-question`: learning flows.
- `jarso/[conversationId]`: an old chat.

## 2. Money actions in domain terms

Source: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/domain.md` (lines 7–40). **Spend** is cash the child holds. **Save** is money the parent keeps. Moving between the jars is a *request*, not income.

- **purchase** (`features/diary/purchase-sheet.tsx`): records cash the child already spent, taken off Spend. No approval. It goes through the offline outbox and has Undo.
  - Copy: "What did you spend?" / "It comes out of your Spend jar." / "Amount" / "What was it for?" + about 9 category chips / "Name it (optional)" / a hint ("What snack?") / button "Write it down" / "Pick what it was for". Success: "Written down. Nice one." with Undo and Done. **About 40 words.**
- **put-in** (`features/requests/put-in-sheet.tsx`): a savings *deposit*. The child physically gives cash to the parent to move it Spend→Save.
  - It stays `pending` until the parent confirms they got the cash. It posts at once only if an auto-confirm rule covers it or the parent chose "just tell me" (notify).
  - While pending, the amount is "held" in Spend.
  - Copy: "Put money into Save" / "Spend has ₹X" / rule lines (e.g. "You can move money between your jars without asking. Your parent gets a note.") / "Amount" / PredictStep / button "Put in".
  - Results: "Waiting for your parent to confirm" / "Added to Save" / "Added to Save. Your parent knows."
  - **About 15 words, rising to about 80 with rules and the predict step.**
- **take-out** (`requests/take-out-sheet.tsx`): a savings *withdrawal*, asking for parent-held Save money as cash. Flow: `pending` → `approved` → `handed_over`, and it posts only at handover. The amount is reserved meanwhile.
  - Copy: "Take money out of Save" / "You can take out up to ₹X" / rule lines ("You can take out at most…", "…without asking") / "Amount" / PredictStep / "What's it for?" / button "Ask".
  - Results: "Sent to your parent" / "Approved, your parent will hand it over" / "Done. Your parent knows."
  - **About 20–80 words.**
- **received** (`diary/received-sheet.tsx`): a gift or other income the child records. It counts at once, with no approval. The child picks the jar; choosing Save shows "Give the cash to your parent to keep."
  - Copy: "What did you get?" / "It goes into your jar right away." / "Amount" / "Where did it come from?" + chips / "Which jar?" Spend|Save / "Name it (optional)" / "Who or what was it?" / "Write it down" / "Pick where it came from".
  - **About 50 words.**
- **ask-extra** (`requests/ask-extra-sheet.tsx`): an emergency grant. Same approve → handover flow, and it lands in Spend.
  - Copy: "Ask for extra money" / "Say what it's for. If your parent says yes, it goes into Spend when they hand it over." / "Amount" / "What's it for?" / "Ask". **About 25 words.**
- **requests** (`requests/request-list.tsx`): the history of take-out, put-in and extra requests. A Waiting one can be cancelled.
  - Copy: "What you asked for". Each row: "Take out | ₹X | Waiting/Said yes/Done/Not now/Cancelled", then date · reason and "Cancel". Empty state: "Nothing here yet. When you move money or ask for extra, it shows up here."
  - **Home links here as "Move my money", but this page is a history list and moves nothing.**
- **goal** (`goals/goal-screen.tsx`, `goal-staircase.tsx`): header "⚽ Boots, ₹450.", an SVG staircase, a caption ("About N allowances to go" / "Reached. Time to ask your parent."), a toggle "The amounts", and the button "Change my goal". **About 20 words.**
- **PredictStep** (`goals/predict-step.tsx`, used by put-in and take-out): "If you put this in, what happens to your goal?" with Further off / Stays the same / Closer. It then shows "That's it." or "Not quite." plus an explanation, "Try another amount. Nothing moves until you decide.", a −/+ stepper and "Use this amount". **About 40 extra words.**
- **Shared sheet parts**: `src/components/sheet/sheet-parts.tsx` (AmountInput, ReasonInput, RuleLines, SubmitButton, SentToast). Success results show as an Android toast through `flash()`, not a celebration.

## 3. Home cards, in order (`home-screen.tsx`)

1. **ScreenHeader**: date line, "Hi, {nickname}!", and an avatar button that opens Me.
2. **PushPermissionCard**: "Notifications are off" / "Turn them on to hear when money arrives or a parent answers." / "Turn on".
3. **JarsCard** (`features/jars/jars-card.tsx`):
   - "Your jars" with the total.
   - Two small boxes drawn with Views, labelled "Spend" and "Save" with their amounts. The captions "Cash you have" and "Kept by your parent" are accessibility hints only, not visible.
   - Notes: "₹X to Save · give it to your parent", "₹X · Waiting to come out".
   - Buttons: **"Spent something?"** and **"Put some in Save"**.
4. **PendingPurchases** (`diary/pending-purchases.tsx`, only if any): "Not sent yet", then each item with its amount and a waiting line, or Change it / Ask a parent / Remove.
5. **HomeStepCard** (`goals/home-step-card.tsx`): one of:
   - A learning NextStepCard: "Next step", e.g. "Day 2. {title}" / "Two or three minutes. Hints are free." / "Start mission".
   - "Do this next" + "Your parent said yes" / "₹20 from Save, coming soon."
   - "Something to save for?" + "Set a goal".
   - "Goal reached" / "Saved up. ⚽ Boots." / "You did it by saving a bit each time." / "Ask to use my savings".
   - "All done for today. Your jars are up to date. Come back any time."
6. **GoalCard** (`goals/goal-card.tsx`): "⚽ Boots" + "See it ›", "₹34 of ₹60", a gold bar, and "About 3 allowances to go". With no goal: "Your goal" / "Something to save for? Pick it and see the climb." / "Set a goal".
7. **List card**: "Take some out", "Got money", "Ask for extra", "Move my money" (which opens requests).
8. **JarGraph** (`jars/jar-graph.tsx`), "Your money ride":
   - Period picker: This week / This month / 3 months.
   - Legend, then an SVG two-line chart with ₹ y-labels, date ticks and gold coin dots.
   - Caption, e.g. "Spend dipped ₹40 and Save climbed ₹20 this week. Nice."
   - "See the numbers" toggle.
   - The web version has a coach riding the track (`riderTrack`); mobile has none.

Home shows about 120–180 words, plus 6 action labels spread across two places.

## 4. Mascot and illustrations

The mobile app has **no drawn mascot at all**. Its only image is the PNG `@jarso/brand/assets/jarso-mark.png`, used on sign-in and as the app icon. `src/components/screen/coach-says.tsx` shows the coach's name and a text bubble only.

On the web, in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/web/src/components/brand/`:
- `kid-figure.tsx` (257 lines): the shared kid drawing.
- `coach-figure.tsx`: `CoachFigure` and `CoachHead`, with cheer and nod reactions.
- `coaches.ts`: the four coaches Pip, Bo, Zuzu and Nell.
- `jarso-eyes.tsx`: the jar with eyes, including a thinking animation.
- `jar-art.tsx` and `jar-outline.ts`: a drawn jar with coins.
- `status-chip.tsx`, `jarso-mark.tsx`, `jarso-wordmark.tsx`.
- Also `features/landing/components/kid-art.tsx`.

**Portability:** these are plain SVG elements (`g`, `ellipse`, `path`, `circle`, `rect`), so they map directly to react-native-svg.
- Blocker 1: colours are Tailwind classes (`fill-teal-800`, `fill-hair-1`, `stroke-kid-ink`). They would need swapping for `fill={tokens…}` or `palette` values. The `skin`, `hair`, `blush` and `kid-ink` colours already exist in `packages/brand/tokens.ts`.
- Blocker 2: the animations are CSS keyframes (`motion-safe:animate-[coach-cheer…]`, `.jar-drop`, `.eyes-lids`) and would need Reanimated.

`/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/mobile-ui-parity.md` (lines 37–47) already lists this as gap #1 (coaches), #4 (eyes), #5 (drawn jars) and #6 (status chip), with a plan to put them in a new `src/components/brand/`.

## 5. Theme and tokens

- **Theme**: `apps/mobile/src/lib/theme.ts` (NAV_THEME and `useTokens()`, both from `@jarso/brand/tokens` semantic colours). `apps/mobile/tailwind.config.js` turns the tokens into CSS variables. `apps/mobile/global.css` only holds the Tailwind directives.

- **Libraries** (`apps/mobile/package.json`):

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ `packages/core/package.json:30`: the `./mascot` export is added.
- ✅ `packages/core/src/mascot/jarso.ts:68-71`: the six moods and five lid colours match the constraints. There is no sad mood.
- ✅ `jarso.ts:77-83`: wear unlock levels are none 1, cap 1, shades 3, headphones 4, crown 5. `availableWear` relies on the object's key order, and `availableWear(1)` gives `["none","cap"]`.
- ✅ `jarso.ts:85-105`: `DEFAULT_LOOK` and `parseLook` are present. Each field falls back on its own, and a non-object falls back to a fresh copy of the default.
- ✅ `jarso.ts:108-122`: `jarsoFill` is clamped to [0,1] in core. Negative save gives 0 and a goal overshoot gives 1. Without a goal it is save ÷ (spend + save), or 0 when both are empty. This covers the Review Focus item.
- ✅ `jarso.ts:124-153`: the moment-to-mood map matches the brief entry for entry.
- ✅ `geometry.ts`: `JAR_OUTLINE_D` is the exact string. `JAR_INSIDE_D` and `JAR_FILL_RANGE` match the brief.
- ✅ `packages/core/src/mascot/index.ts`: the barrel re-exports both modules.
- ✅ Purity: the only import is zod, with no React, `@/**` or platform globals.
- ✅ Tests cover every case listed in the brief.
- ⚠️ Not verifiable from the diff: whether tests and lint pass, and whether zod 4 `.catch` covers a missing key. The `parseLook(null)` test only exercises the whole-object fallback.

### Strengths
- Core stays pure and the code is small.
- The clamp lives in core, not in the renderer.
- `moodFor` uses a `Record<JarsoMoment, JarsoMood>`, so the compiler forces every moment to have a mood.
- `parseLook` returns a copy, so callers can't mutate the shared default.
- A goal target of 0 gives 0 instead of dividing by zero.

### Issues
#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None.

#### Minor
- `jarso.ts:74` and `jarso.ts:99`: `Wear` is a hand-written union that is separate from the `WEAR_UNLOCK_LEVEL` keys. The zod enum is built from `Object.keys` with a cast. Deriving the type from a single `WEARS` const tuple, as is done for `LID_COLORS`, would remove the cast and the chance of drift.
- `jarso.ts:87`: `DEFAULT_LOOK` is a mutable exported object. Add `Readonly<JarsoLook>` or `Object.freeze`.
- `jarso.test.ts:211-223`: the moment list is typed by hand, so a newly added moment won't be tested. Export a `JARSO_MOMENTS` const or use `Object.keys` on the map. Nothing tests a goal target of 0, a missing field in `parseLook`, or the non-clamped mapping values. The brief didn't require these.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and global constraints exactly. The fill clamp is in core and tested, and core stays pure. The remaining issues are minor polish.

### Strengths
- The resolver is scoped to `/node_modules/<pkg>/` basedirs and relative requests inside Reanimated. That avoids the shipped resolver's bug of matching `react-native-worklets` in pnpm folder names. The header comment states this reason well.
- It touches only the `extensions` option and delegates to `defaultResolver`, so it is minimal and non-invasive.

#### Critical
None.

- `tests/reanimated-resolver.js:84-97`: the `WEB_ONLY_IN_JEST` list is tied to Reanimated 4.7.1's internal file names, and the basename match on generic names like `util` and `mappers` is broad. It is scoped to Reanimated-internal relative requests, so the blast radius is small. The failure mode on a Reanimated or Expo SDK upgrade is a confusing worklets init error. Fix: add a one-line comment saying "re-check on Reanimated upgrade".
- `tests/reanimated-resolver.js:106`: the `/node_modules/<pkg>/` match assumes POSIX separators. Fine on Linux and macOS, but it would silently not apply on Windows. Low impact.
- `jest.config.js:25`: the new comment line is appended under the pnpm and lucide comment block, in front of an unrelated `resolver` key. It reads as part of the lucide note. Fix: put a blank line before it, or move it to sit directly above `resolver`.
- `tests/lib/reanimated-mock.test.tsx`: the test only proves that `Animated.View` renders. Nothing exercises the `expo-audio` mock or the `useReducedMotion`/`withSequence` paths that later tasks depend on. A second `it` calling `createAudioPlayer().play()` and `useReducedMotion()` would make the harness claim verified. This is optional, since the brief specified only one test.
- `setup.ts:134`: the comment says the resolver "picks its web build". More precisely, it picks the JS non-`.native` files. This is a wording nit.

**Reasoning:** The brief's deliverables are met, and the resolver deviation is justified and carefully scoped, with no concrete mis-resolution risk found. The remaining items are polish.

Implementation:
- The setting lives in a module variable. `playSound` treats it as on until `getSoundOn()` has read AsyncStorage, then respects the saved value.
- `getSoundOn()` reads the key `jarso.sound`; "off" means off, anything else or empty means on.
- One lazily created player per clip, reused with `seekTo(0)` then `play()`.
- `playSound` wraps everything in try/catch and logs failures with `logger.warn`.
- `useSoundOn` subscribes to a listener set, so several switches stay in sync.

Concerns:
- `pnpm --filter @jarso/mobile typecheck` fails with TS2307 in `tests/components/jarso.test.tsx`: `@/components/brand` is not found. That is the parallel agent's in-progress work, not mine. I saw no errors in my files.
- Because sound counts as on until the setting is read, a user with sound off could hear the very first clip if it fires before the storage read finishes. Fixing that means warming `getSoundOn()` at app start; I did not wire that.
- The script writes with `process.stdout.write` because Biome bans `console`.

Intended commit message: feat(mobile): short sound effects with an off switch

chars: 4117

### Spec Compliance
- Present and matching the brief:
  - Generator `scripts/make-sounds.mjs` uses Node built-ins only. Frequencies, durations and 16-bit mono 22.05 kHz match the brief. Clips are 140, 90 and 440 ms, all under 1 s.
  - `SoundName`, `getSoundOn`, `setSoundOn`, `playSound` and `useSoundOn` are exported. The storage key is `jarso.sound`, and the default is on.
  - There is one lazy player per clip, reused via `seekTo(0)` then `play()`. The barrel exports it. The `expo-audio` plugin is added to `app.config.ts`.
  - `playSound` wraps everything in try/catch and logs with `logger.warn`. `console.log` is not used.
  - The four brief tests plus one extra are present.
- ⚠️ Not run: I did not execute the tests. I did not check that `tests/setup.ts` mocks `expo-audio`. The report claims they pass.
- Violation of the global constraint "sound is off when the saved setting is off":
  - `sound.ts:19` and `sound.ts:48-49` treat sound as on until storage has been read.
  - `playSound` starts `getSoundOn()` without awaiting it, then plays anyway. So a child who turned sound off hears the first clip after each cold start.
  - The implementer flagged this as a concern. The brief said "warmed by `getSoundOn()` on first use", which does not excuse a constraint violation.

### Strengths
- `playSound` is synchronous and cannot throw from its own code paths.
- A failed `createAudioPlayer` stores no player, so the next call retries.
- Storage read and write failures are caught and logged.
- `useSoundOn` cleans up its listener and guards against setting state after unmount.
- The tests use `isolateModules`, so the module-level state does not leak between tests.
- The generator is small and self-contained. It has a 3 ms attack ramp to avoid clicks, and it prints a usage message on bad args.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important
- `apps/mobile/src/lib/sound.ts:48-51`: this plays a clip even when the saved setting is off, as described above.
  - Fix: when `soundOn === null`, do not play synchronously. Run `void getSoundOn().then((on) => { if (on) play(name); })`, with the play logic in a shared inner function. Also warm it at app start, for example by calling `getSoundOn()` in the root layout, so the async path is rare.
  - Add a test: save "off", call `playSound` without awaiting `getSoundOn` first, flush the microtasks, and assert that `play` and `createAudioPlayer` were not called.

#### Minor
- `sound.ts:54-55`: in expo-audio, `seekTo` is async, so it returns a Promise.
  - A rejection would escape the try/catch as an unhandled rejection.
  - `play()` can also run before the seek finishes.
  - Fix: `void Promise.resolve(player.seekTo(0)).catch(...)`, or chain `.then(() => player.play())` inside the guarded block. ⚠️ I assumed the expo-audio signature from memory and did not check it.
- `sound.ts:21-29`: `getSoundOn` can overwrite a newer `setSoundOn` value.
  - A `getItem` that was already in flight when `setSoundOn(false)` ran can resolve afterwards. It would then set `soundOn` back to true.
  - Fix: use a version counter, or skip assigning if `setSoundOn` ran in the meantime.
- `tests/lib/sound.test.ts`:
  - "silent when off" only asserts `createAudioPlayer` was not called. It does not check `play`.
  - "never throws" does not assert that `logger.warn` was called.
  - Nothing covers off persisted in storage together with `playSound`, which is the case that fails (see Important).
- `apps/mobile/src/lib/index.ts`: the barrel header says platform-bound modules are not exported from it. `sound` now brings `expo-audio` and AsyncStorage into every `@/lib` consumer. The brief asked for this, so it is acceptable, but the comment is now slightly misleading.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** `playSound` never throws, and the assets, generator, API and plugin all match the brief. But the first clip after a cold start plays even when the saved setting is off, which violates the binding "sound is off when the setting is off" constraint, and no test covers it. A small fix to the first-play path, plus a test, should be enough.

The four named risks:

- **Blink and reduced motion:** honoured. There is no timer under reduced motion, and the face is drawn still. The test confirms it.
- **Clip id collisions:** harmless. Every instance uses `jarso-inside` with identical geometry, and each `Svg` keeps its own defs, so this causes no visual bug.

### Strengths

- Fill is clamped to 0–1. The a11y props are built as a typed `as const` union.
- The tests cover fill position, every mood, the crown, the blink timing and reduced motion.

### Issues
#### Critical

#### Important

### Spec Compliance
All brief items are present:
- **Interfaces match the brief.** The `Celebration` type, `CelebrationProvider`, `useCelebrate()` and `Confetti({ count = 40 })` are all there.
- **Queue:** one celebration shows at a time (`queue[0]`), first in first out.
- **On show:** sound (default `cheer`), Success haptic and an announcement of the title. These fire once per celebration because the effect is keyed on `current?.id`.
- **Primary button:** closes first, then runs `onPress` in the effect after the celebration has left the screen.
- **Modal:** transparent and `statusBarTranslucent`. The `sheet: true` layout and the full-screen gradient layout are both there.
- **Gradients:** teal 600→900, gold `#fff3b0`→gold-100, grape 500→900, success `success-soft`→`card` (per the ruling).
- **Confetti:** 40 pieces in palette colours, falls once over 1.6 s, returns null under reduced motion.
- **Mood:** defaults to `cheer`.
- **Child layout:** `CelebrationProvider` wraps the authenticated branch. The guards are unchanged.
- **Sound warm-up:** `void getSoundOn()` runs once on mount.

No raw hex except the brief-mandated `#fff3b0`. The flame chip uses `palette.blush`, per the ruling.

Review focus, both confirmed:
- **Reduced motion:** the celebration still shows and still announces, with 0 confetti pieces. The test is meaningful: the default case asserts 40 pieces, so the `useReducedMotion` spy really flips the result. Jarso (`src/components/brand/jarso.tsx:58-84`) checks `useReducedMotion` itself, so he is drawn still regardless of the `bounce` prop.
- **Back-to-back calls:** they queue and never stack. The "queues two" test checks one announce, the second title hidden, then shown after "Nice".

I ran `pnpm exec jest --maxWorkers=2 tests/components/celebration.test.tsx`: 10/10 passed.

### Strengths
- **Queue logic holds up.** Ids come from a monotonic counter, so `closing.current` needs no reset. The `closing` guard stops double taps and a back press during close from running `onPress` twice. `setQueue` uses a functional update with `q[0]?.id === id`, so there are no stale-closure problems. `celebrate` is a stable `useCallback([])`, so the context value never changes.
- **`onPress` that calls `celebrate()` itself works.** `then()` runs inside the id-change effect, and calling `celebrate` there appends to the queue, which advances it. No re-entrancy problem.
- **Android back is safe.** `onRequestClose` → `close(id)` with no callback, so the queue advances and nothing gets stuck.
- **Fresh confetti per celebration.** The view is keyed by id, so each one gets a new burst. Confetti uses only Reanimated shared values and no JS timers, so unmounting cleans up. Hooks run before the reduced-motion early return, so hook order is stable.
- **Tests are behavioural.** They check what is shown, the announcements, and that `onPress` runs only after the celebration is gone (`openWhenPressed === false`). They also cover the same object queued twice.

### Issues
#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None that blocks the task. The item below is a device-verification gap, not a proven bug.

#### Minor
1. **`apps/mobile/app/(child)/_layout.tsx:45`: React Native `Modal` over a native `formSheet`, not verified on a device.** The comment says "Celebrations show over the whole child area, sheets included", and the obvious triggers live in `SHEETS` (`purchase`, `put-in`, `take-out`, `received`, `goal-sheet`). The provider sits outside the `Stack`. On iOS, a React Native `Modal` presented while a react-native-screens `formSheet` is already up has historically failed: UIKit logs "already presenting" and nothing appears. Jest cannot catch this. **Fix:** check on an iOS device, with `celebrate()` fired from inside a sheet, before Tasks 8+ wire it in. If it fails, have callers dismiss the sheet first and then celebrate, and change the comment.
2. **`celebration.tsx:330-340`: one throwing callback breaks the next celebration.** If `then()` (a caller's `onPress`) throws, the sound, haptic and announcement for the next queued celebration are skipped. **Fix:** run `then` after the show side effects, or in its own `try`/`catch` that logs through `logger`.
3. **`celebration.tsx:359`: Android back skips the primary action.** Back dismisses without calling `primary.onPress`. That is fine for "Nice"-style closes. But for lesson feedback (`sheet`, "Continue" moves to the next question), back would leave the child on the old question. **Fix:** decide on purpose. Either back runs the primary action for sheets, or document that callers must not rely on `onPress` happening.
4. **`celebration.tsx:369`: `useCelebrate()` throws outside the provider.** The parent area has no provider, so a component shared between child and parent that calls it would crash the parent screens. Today only child screens are meant to call it, so throwing is defensible. A no-op fallback, or a note in the hook's JSDoc that it is child-only, would make the boundary explicit.
5. **Test gap: no test where `onPress` calls `celebrate()`.** This is a re-entrant path the queue design should guarantee, and it is cheap to add.
6. **`celebration-view.tsx:218`: `bounce={!reduced}` does nothing.** Per the report, Jarso hops only on a mood change, and he already checks reduced motion himself. The prop is redundant. Either drop it or make Jarso hop on mount if a hop is wanted.
7. **`celebration-view.tsx:184`: chip background is always light.** It uses `semantic.light.card` on every tone, including `success`, whose gradient follows the colour scheme. In dark mode that gives white pills on a dark card. This is probably intended (pills look the same everywhere); confirm against the mockup.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The spec and both review-focus behaviours are implemented correctly and covered by meaningful tests. The queue has no stale-closure, double-fire or stuck-on-back problems. The remaining items are small hardening or design decisions, plus one iOS check on a real device: a `Modal` opened from inside a `formSheet` screen.

## Review: Mobile kid rework, Plan 1 (Foundation)

Scope: the whole of `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-1-foundation/review-final.diff`, `progress.md`, the plan and the spec. I also read these at source: the expo-audio 58.0.11 config plugin and its Android focus code, `metro.config.js`, `app.json`, and the two `CoachSays` call sites. I made no changes to the tree.

**Open-handle check.** Command: `jest --maxWorkers=2 --detectOpenHandles tests/components tests/lib/sound.test.ts tests/lib/reanimated-mock.test.tsx`. 8 suites and 48 tests passed, no open handles were reported, and Jest exited cleanly. The branch's code supports that: Jarso's blink interval and timeout are cleared on unmount, RNTL unmounts after each test, the Reanimated mock finishes at once, and the sound promises settle. This branch probably did not cause the "did not exit" warning; it most likely comes from a suite that was already there.

### Strengths
- `packages/core/src/mascot` stays pure (only zod). `jarsoFill` is clamped in core and again in `Jarso`, which covers Review Focus 1. `parseLook` falls back field by field.
- The celebration queue is solid:
  - Ids are unique, so the same celebration twice shows twice.
  - A second tap on the way out is ignored (`closing` ref).
  - `onPress` runs only after the celebration has left the screen.
  - Reduced motion still announces and renders no confetti.
- Tests cover Review Focus 2–4.
- `playSound` never throws, including the async `seekTo` rejection. It waits for the saved setting instead of guessing, and the `version` counter stops a slow read from overwriting a newer choice.
- The custom Reanimated resolver is well reasoned. Reanimated's own resolver over-matches pnpm paths.
- The keycap refactor (the `KEYCAPS` list plus compound heights) is clean. The ruling that moves the dark `keycap-ledge-white` to `#05121a` fixes a real defect in the plan (Review Focus 5).
- Sound assets load through `require`, and Metro's default `assetExts` include `wav`. `metro.config.js` does not override them, so the clips will be bundled.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important

**1. `apps/mobile/app.config.ts:60`: the bare `"expo-audio"` plugin adds a microphone permission and a background playback service.**
- What: with default options (`recordAudioAndroid = true`, `enableBackgroundPlayback = true`) the plugin adds `RECORD_AUDIO`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK`, and registers `AudioControlsService` (foregroundServiceType `mediaPlayback`) in the manifest. `app.json`'s `permissions` list only adds permissions, so it does not stop these.
- Why it matters: this is a children's app. A microphone permission with no use breaks rule 15 (collect minimum data) and draws Play Families / Data-safety scrutiny. The media-playback foreground service type also needs its own Play Console declaration.
- Fix: the plugin isn't needed for foreground playback (the module's own manifest only asks for `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS`), so drop it. If it stays, use `["expo-audio", { recordAudioAndroid: false, enableBackgroundPlayback: false }]`.
- A native rebuild is required either way.

**2. `apps/mobile/src/components/brand/jarso.tsx:562-563` (with `jarso-parts.tsx` `WearArt` "shades"): the shades are hidden behind the eyes.**
- What: `WearArt` is drawn before `Face`. The lenses (x 170–254, y 226–290) sit almost entirely under the white eye ellipses (x 176–248, y 212–304).
- Effect: in happy, wow, think and wave, the shades show only as dark slivers around white eyes. They read properly only in cheer and sleepy, where the eyes are lines.
- Cause: the plan's draw order puts wear before the eyes, and the mockup has no shades to check against.
- Fix: draw face-worn items (shades) after `Face`, for example by splitting `WearArt` into head wear and face wear. Add a test that the shades node comes after the eyes. This is cheap now and is needed before Plan 6 exposes the Lv 3 unlock.

**3. `apps/mobile/src/components/celebrate/celebration.tsx:856` (`onDismiss={() => close(current.id)}`): Android back closes a celebration without its action.**
- What: the hardware back button closes the celebration but never runs `primary.onPress`. This is the "deferred minor" in the ledger, but it shapes the API that Plans 3 and 5 build on.
- Example: in Plan 3, a flow's success celebration whose primary action closes the purchase sheet would leave the child, after pressing back, on a filled-in flow they could submit again (a duplicate log). The Plan 5 lesson sheet has the same gap with "Continue".
- Fix: settle it now. Either:
  - back runs `primary.onPress` by default (or an explicit `onClose` field on `Celebration`), or
  - document in `useCelebrate`'s JSDoc that callers must finish their navigation before calling `celebrate()`.
- Add a test either way.

#### Minor
- **`celebration.tsx:829-831`, a throwing `onPress` can crash the child area.** `then?.()` runs inside `useEffect`. A throw there goes to the nearest error boundary, which takes down the child area, rather than just skipping the next celebration's sound, haptic and announcement as the ledger says. Fix: wrap it in try/catch with `logger.warn` before Plan 3 wires real callbacks.
- **`coach-says.tsx:1004` with `features/news/news-screen.tsx:53-55`, invisible bubble in Notifications.** The bubble is `bg-card` with a tail coloured `card`. In the Notifications empty state it sits inside a `<Card>`, so the bubble and its tail vanish and only the 3 dp bottom edge shows. Before this change it was `bg-secondary`. Fix: give the bubble a tone or surface prop, or drop the wrapping `Card` there.
- **`celebration-view.tsx:709-716`, the celebration's Jarso never bounces.** Each celebration is keyed and its mood never changes, so `bounce` never fires. design.md §8 implies a bounce. Fix: add a hop on mount, or drop the prop and the doc claim.
- **`celebration-view.tsx:751-776`, safe-area insets are ignored.** The `Modal` has `statusBarTranslucent`, and the layout uses fixed `pt-20`/`pb-8`/`pb-10`. Under Android 15+ edge-to-edge the sheet's buttons may sit under the gesture bar or leave a strip. Fix: use `useSafeAreaInsets()`. Unverified, since no device was available.
- **`src/lib/index.ts:1278`, `./sound` in the `@/lib` barrel.** The barrel's own header says platform-bound modules are imported directly. `sound.ts` pulls in expo-audio's native module and AsyncStorage for every importer of `@/lib` (112 files, parent area included). There is no expo-updates, so no OTA crash risk, but it breaks the convention. Fix: import it as `@/lib/sound` directly (and list it in the header), or update the comment.
- **`sound.ts`, every clip briefly pauses the child's music.** With no audio mode set, expo-audio asks Android for `AUDIOFOCUS_GAIN_TRANSIENT`. Fix: set the audio mode once to duck or mix with other audio. The exact option name in expo-audio 58 still needs checking.
- **Raw hex in mobile components.** `#fff3b0` (`celebration-view.tsx:618`, which the plan prescribed) and `#ffffff` (`button.tsx:1229`) go against the "no raw hex" constraint. Fix: add tokens when convenient.
- **`jarso.tsx:526`, NaN fill.** A `NaN` fill passes the clamp and gives `y=NaN`. Fix: guard with `Number.isFinite`.
- **`docs/design.md` §8, stale row.** It still says "keycap drops 2 px", while the phone's keycap now drops 5 dp.

### Deferred-minor triage
**Fix before merge (all cheap):**
- T7: a throwing `onPress` (above; it is a crash path, not just skipped side effects).
- T7: Android back skips `primary.onPress` (Important #3): decide the API, or at least document it.
- T6: the barrel comment is now misleading. Fix it together with the `@/lib/sound` minor.
- T1: add a "re-check on Reanimated upgrade" comment to the resolver's `WEB_ONLY_IN_JEST` (one line; protects every later upgrade).

**Can wait:**
- T3 group: the `Wear` union not derived from a tuple, the mutable `DEFAULT_LOOK`, and the missing tests for goal 0 and a missing `parseLook` field. Pick these up in Plan 2 or 6 when progress or customisation uses them.
- T2: the literal `#ffffff` spinner.
- T1: the POSIX-only path match (no Windows developers), comment placement, and the harness test covering only `Animated.View`.
- T4 group:
  - The StrictMode bounce guard (dev only, and expo-router doesn't wrap StrictMode).
  - Blinks in lockstep, and the sleepy "z" drawn in the mouth group.
  - Repeated inline palette values, and the shared clip id (react-native-svg scopes ids to each `Svg`).
- T6: a failed first storage read defaults sound to on. That is harmless.
- T7 rest:
  - `useCelebrate` throws outside its provider: document it as child-only, and revisit in Plan 10 if shared components call it.
  - No test for an `onPress` that calls `celebrate()` again. Add it with the try/catch fix.
  - The redundant bounce prop is covered by the Minor above.
  - The chip background is always the light card. That is fine; it is explicit and legible.

**Parked items:**
- RN `Modal` over a native formSheet on iOS: fine to keep parked, since the app is Android-only.
- Skipped device check: Android behaviour is also unverified (keycap 5 dp press, Modal over formSheet, edge-to-edge insets, bubble in a card, shades). Do a quick device pass before merge, or flag it explicitly in the PR as planned.

### Declined to judge
- iOS behaviour (Modal over formSheet, the silent switch, `UIBackgroundModes` added by the plugin): the app ships on Android only, per the ruling.
- Whether TalkBack actually reads the announcement once the Modal takes focus: this needs a device, and the plan only asks that the call is made.
- How close the visuals are to the mockup (colours, proportions, gradients, headphone placement): the device step was skipped by ruling.
- Changes to the mockup HTML: it is design material, not product code.
- The choice of lid hexes, gold-200 against the mockup's `#fdc300`, and grape-600 on the keycap face: these are recorded controller rulings with their reasons.
- Whether the sounds are pleasant: the spec leaves that to the builder; only "under 1 s" binds, and they are.
- Splitting the work into commits: there are no commits yet, by the user's rule.
- Jest open handles in suites this branch didn't add: outside the change.
- Web impact of the new tokens in `tokens.css`: they are additive and the web doesn't use them.

### Recommendations (for later plans' fit)
- **A look context.** Jarso's look and fill are passed per instance. Plan 6 customisation would then have to thread `look` into every `CoachSays` and every `celebrate()` call. Add a `JarsoLookProvider` (child look plus Save fill) that `Jarso` reads by default, in Plan 2 or 6.
- **`CoachSays` props.** It needs optional `mood`, or a `JarsoMoment` routed through `moodFor`, plus `look` and `fill`, for the Home hero bubble and the moment cards in Plan 3.
- **The wrong-answer sheet in Plan 5.** `Celebration` always plays a sound (cheer by default), fires a Success haptic and shows confetti in the sheet. The calm "Close! Try again" sheet needs `confetti: false`, a calm tone, and a quieter or no sound and haptic. Extend the type, or use a separate sheet.
- **Sparks and streak ticking up** (spec, Flows → Success): chips are static labels today. Plan 3 should add a ticking-number chip.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge?** With fixes.

**Reasoning:** The foundation is well built and its API fits the later plans. Two fixes are cheap and needed: the expo-audio plugin adds a microphone permission and a media foreground service to a children's app, and the shades are drawn under the eyes. The Android-back behaviour of `celebrate()` and the crash path from a throwing callback should be settled before Plan 3 builds flows on them.

Design (do not write files) the database part of "Plan 2: progress backend" for Jarso's mobile kid rework. Repo: /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso. Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-design.md — read sections "Fun loop (engagement without manipulation)" and "Data and backend implications". Also read docs/database.md, docs/domain.md and CLAUDE.md rules 7, 8, 14, 15, and look at recent migrations in supabase/migrations (e.g. 20261106100000_welcome_tour.sql, the push ones) and supabase/tests/database for pgTAP conventions.

Requirements from the spec:
- Sparks ⚡ (never money, never convertible): fixed amounts — log a purchase or money got +10; daily check-in +5; a mission step +15; a save (Spend→Save deposit) +20; goal milestone 25/50/75/100% +25 each; goal reached +100. Daily caps stop farming (at most 3 logs count per day). Awarded server-side in the same DB functions that record the action, so the client can't farm.
- Levels: Sparks thresholds; each unlocks a Jarso look (outfits none/cap Lv1, shades Lv3, headphones Lv4, crown Lv5 — rules in packages/core/src/mascot).
- ~20 badges for habits (first save, 7-day streak, goal reached, diary a week…).
- Streak: any check-in, log or mission on a (family-timezone) day counts; one automatic rest day per week covers a miss; a broken streak is never mentioned (just restarts at 1).
- Daily check-in "Nope" writes a check-in row only, never a ledger row. "Yes" leads to a purchase log (separately).
- profiles.child_facing_name (what the child calls the parent, e.g. "Mum"; optional short text) — or a family-level field; decide.
- Jarso look stored per child (lid, wear), validated against unlock level.
- RLS: child reads own rows, parent reads their child's; only Postgres functions write.

Find and name the exact existing Postgres functions/triggers that record: a purchase, money received, a deposit request (put-in) being confirmed/posted, a mission step completion, goal progress (where goal % can be computed), and how the family timezone/local date is derived. Decide: award inside those functions vs an AFTER INSERT trigger on the ledger/learning tables (weigh: the spec says "same DB functions"; triggers catch every path incl. web; idempotency on undo/fix of a purchase — should Sparks be clawed back on undo? propose).

Deliver (≤1200 words): table DDL sketch (columns, constraints, indexes), function signatures (name, args, returns, security definer/invoker, search_path), where each award hooks in, cap/streak/rest-day algorithm, undo behaviour, RLS policies per operation, migration file names (timestamp after 20261106100000), the pgTAP test cases to write, and anything risky (concurrency: two awards racing; timezone day boundaries). Note: we use the hosted Supabase project; migrations are pushed only after user approval; pgTAP runs in PR CI.
