## Plan 2 progress backend: database design

I wrote no files and ran no commands against the database. Paths below are relative to `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/`.

### The existing functions that record each action

| Action | Function | What it writes |
|---|---|---|
| Purchase | `record_purchase` → `record_purchase_at` (`supabase/migrations/20261102000000_diary_complete.sql:293` / `:198`) | `ledger_entries` row: kind `purchase`, source `diary`, mode `recorded`, key `purchase:<child>:<key>`, under the ledger lock |
| Money got | `record_money_received` → `_at` (same file `:418` / `:339`) | kind `gift_in`, source `diary`, jar spend or save |
| Undo / fix | `undo_diary_entry(_at)` `:536/:447` (`undo_purchase` `:549` wraps it); `correct_diary_entry(_at)` `:721/:568` | Undo writes a `reversal` row. A fix writes a reversal plus a new row with `corrects_entry_id` set |
| Save (put-in) | `create_deposit` (`20261102123022_jar_moves_notify.sql:260`, auto-confirm path) and `decide_deposit` (`20261031020001_request_functions.sql:430`) → `complete_request` → **`post_request`** (latest: `20261031060000_emergency_funds.sql`, around `:172`) | `deposit_out` + `deposit_in` with keys `request:<id>:out\|in`, status `confirmed`. `post_request` is the only place a deposit is posted |
| Mission step | `record_step_result` (`20261030100000_learning_progress.sql:256`) | `step_results` row, under the attempt row lock. `complete_mission` (latest `20261030130002_device_occurred_at.sql`) sets attempt status `completed` |
| Goal % | `goal_progress(child)` (`20261031023826_goals.sql:186`) | Counted = `balance(save) − reserved_save`. It sets `reached_at` lazily, on read. Nothing stores the percentage |
| Family date | `family_today(family_id, now)` (`20261029130002_allowance_schedules.sql:107`) | `(now at time zone families.timezone)::date`. It is security invoker |

Do not reuse `learning_checkins`. It holds the one-off FR10 starting check-in, not the daily one.

### Where awards hook in: AFTER INSERT triggers, not edits to the functions

Recommendation: use triggers. A trigger fires in the same transaction and stays server-side. Clients have no write grants on these tables, so it meets the spec's intent of "can't be farmed". It also catches every path, including web and parent-confirmed deposits.

The alternative is re-creating `record_purchase_at`, `record_money_received_at`, `post_request` and `record_step_result`. Those are very large bodies to copy, which is more risk and more drift.

The one exception is the new `daily_checkin`, which calls the award function directly.

- **`progress_on_ledger_entry`**: `after insert on ledger_entries for each row when (new.mode = 'recorded' and (new.kind in ('purchase','gift_in','deposit_in') or new.jar = 'save'))`.
  - `purchase`, or `gift_in` with source `diary`, and `corrects_entry_id is null` → log award, key `log:<entry_id>`.
  - `deposit_in` → save award, key `save:<source_id>`.
  - Any recorded save-jar row → `progress_check_goal(child, award => true)`.
- **`progress_on_step_result`**: after insert on `step_results`. Looks up `mission_key` from the attempt. Phase `main` → step award, key `step:<mission_key>:<step_key>`, at most once per step for life, whether right or wrong. Every phase marks the day active.
- **`progress_on_mission_completed`**: after update of `status` on `mission_attempts`, when the new status is `completed`. Updates the missions counter and badges only.
- **`progress_on_goal_change`**: after insert, or update of `target_minor`, on `goals` → `progress_check_goal(child, award => false)`. Milestones the child has already passed are marked with 0 Sparks, so creating a goal or lowering its target pays nothing.

Each trigger body wraps the award in `begin … exception when others then raise warning … end`. A Sparks bug must never block a purchase or deposit. The savepoint costs little at about 60 users, and pgTAP stops bugs from hiding.

### Tables

```sql
child_progress (
  child_id uuid pk references profiles on delete cascade,
  family_id uuid not null references families on delete cascade,
  sparks bigint not null default 0 check (sparks >= 0),
  level smallint not null default 1 check (level between 1 and 20),
  streak int not null default 0 check (streak >= 0), best_streak int not null default 0,
  last_active_day date,
  rest_week date,                                  -- Monday of the week whose rest day is used
  lid text not null default 'cyan' check (lid in ('cyan','grape','blush','green','ink')),
  wear text not null default 'none' check (wear in ('none','cap','shades','headphones','crown')),
  logs_count int, saves_count int, steps_count int, checkins_count int,
  missions_count int, goals_reached_count int,     -- all int not null default 0 check (>= 0); badge counters
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(), updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),  -- + set_updated_at trigger
  constraint child_progress_wear_unlocked check (public.progress_wear_level(wear) <= level)
)

child_activity_days (
  child_id uuid references profiles on delete cascade,
  family_id uuid not null references families on delete cascade,
  day date not null,
  checkin text check (checkin in ('yes','nope')),
  logs smallint not null default 0, saves smallint not null default 0, steps smallint not null default 0,
  goal_awards smallint not null default 0,
  active boolean generated always as (checkin is not null or logs > 0 or steps > 0) stored,
  primary key (child_id, day)
)
-- One row per child per day. Holds the check-in, the per-day cap counters and the streak source.

spark_events (
  id uuid pk default gen_random_uuid(),
  child_id, family_id (FKs, cascade),
  kind text not null check (kind in ('log','checkin','step','save','goal_milestone','goal_reached')),
  amount int not null check (amount between 0 and 100),
  source_key text not null check (char_length(source_key) <= 200),
  day date not null,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  unique (child_id, source_key)
)
-- Audit trail, celebration feed and idempotency. Index (child_id, created_at desc).

child_badges (
  child_id, family_id (FKs, cascade),
  badge_key text check (badge_key ~ '^[a-z0-9_]{1,40}$'),
  earned_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  primary key (child_id, badge_key)
)
```

Every table gets an index on `family_id`. The migration backfills a zero `child_progress` row for every approved child, and `progress_lock` also creates rows lazily.

**`child_facing_name`**: put it on the parent's `profiles` row, not on the family. Two parents are "Mum" and "Dad", so a family-level field cannot hold both.
- Column: `text check (child_facing_name is null or (char_length between 1 and 20 and = btrim))`.
- Add `grant update (child_facing_name) on profiles to authenticated`. The existing policy already limits updates to a parent's own row.
- Children can already read it through "Profiles are viewable by self and family".

### Functions

All are `security definer` with `set search_path = ''`. "Internal" means `revoke execute … from public, anon, authenticated`.

- `progress_level(p_sparks bigint) → smallint` and `progress_wear_level(p_wear text) → smallint`: immutable SQL that mirrors the thresholds in `packages/core/src/progress` and `WEAR_UNLOCK_LEVEL`. The check constraint uses the second one.
- `progress_lock(p_child_id uuid) → child_progress` (internal): `insert … on conflict do nothing`, then `select … for update`. Returns null when the child is not an approved child.
- `progress_award(p_child_id uuid, p_kind text, p_source_key text, p_amount int, p_now timestamptz default now()) → int` (internal):
  1. Lock the progress row.
  2. `day := family_today(family, p_now)`.
  3. Upsert the activity day.
  4. Apply the cap.
  5. `insert spark_events … on conflict do nothing`. If nothing was inserted, return 0.
  6. Add the Sparks, recompute the level, refresh the streak, increment the counter.
  7. `progress_check_badges`.
- `progress_streak(p_child_id uuid, p_today date) → table(streak int, rest_used_this_week boolean)` (internal, stable).
- `progress_check_goal(p_child_id uuid, p_award boolean) → void` and `progress_check_badges(p_child_id uuid, p_day date) → void` (internal).
- `daily_checkin(p_answer text) → jsonb` (child only) → `{status: 'recorded'|'already', sparks}`, plus an internal `daily_checkin_at(p_answer, p_now)` clock seam. It writes `checkin` on today's activity row and awards +5, key `checkin:<day>`. It never writes a ledger row.
- `set_jarso_look(p_lid text, p_wear text) → void` (child only). `22023` for an unknown value, `JA409 'Not unlocked yet'` for a locked look. The check constraint is the backstop.
- `get_progress(p_child_id uuid) → jsonb` (stable; `is_self` or `is_parent_of`, else `JA403`). Returns sparks, level, next threshold, live streak, rest used, look, badges. The streak is computed live because the stored value goes stale after misses.

### Caps (per family-local day, counted from the activity row)

| Kind | Rule |
|---|---|
| Logs (purchase + money got) | 3 per day |
| Check-in | 1 per day |
| Steps | 20 per day, and each step only once for life |
| Saves | 2 per day (stops 1p deposit farming) |
| Goal milestones | Once per goal and milestone (`goal:<id>:25`) |
| Goal reached | At most 1 per rolling 7 days |

Over-cap actions still count toward the streak but leave no `spark_events` row. Silenced milestones (passed before the goal was created or its target lowered) get a 0-Sparks row so they never pay later.

Award day is family-local `now()`, not the device `occurred_at`. An offline log synced the next morning counts today, and backdated logs can't refill a missed day.

### Streak and rest day

Week = ISO Monday, worked out on the family-local date.

```
d := today; if not active(today) then d := today - 1   -- today not done yet is not a miss
streak := 0; covered := {}
loop (at most 400 steps):
  if active(d): streak++, d--
  elsif week(d) not in covered and active(d - 1): covered += week(d), d--   -- rest day: keeps the run, adds nothing
  else exit
rest_used_this_week := week(today) in covered
```

Two misses in a row are never covered. A broken streak just starts again at 1, and there is no "lost" field anywhere. `best_streak` feeds the badges.

### Undo

Sparks are never taken back, because taking them away is punishment (spec anti-goal). Farming stays bounded:
- Reversal rows award nothing.
- A fix (`corrects_entry_id` set) awards nothing.
- An undo does not free up a cap slot.

So undo-and-relog earns at most 30 Sparks a day. When a reversal lowers Save, milestones already won stay won.

### Badges (keys, rules in `progress_check_badges`)

`first_log`, `logs_10`, `logs_50`, `logs_100`, `diary_week` (logs on 7 days in a row), `first_checkin`, `checkins_7`, `checkins_30`, `streak_3`, `streak_7`, `streak_14`, `streak_30`, `first_save`, `saves_5`, `saves_20`, `first_mission`, `missions_5`, `missions_10`, `goal_set`, `goal_half`, `goal_reached`, `level_3`, `level_5`.

### RLS

| Table | select | insert / update / delete |
|---|---|---|
| `child_progress`, `child_badges`, `spark_events` | `(select is_self(child_id)) or (select is_parent_of(child_id))` | Explicit deny policies (goals style) |
| `child_activity_days` | The child only (`is_self`); parents get the streak through `get_progress`. This keeps the minimum of the child's data visible | Explicit deny policies |

All four: `revoke all from anon` and `revoke insert, update, delete, truncate from authenticated`.

### Migrations

1. `20261106120000_child_facing_name.sql`
2. `20261106120100_progress.sql`: tables, RLS, the level and wear mirrors, the lock and award functions, the streak, badges, backfill.
3. `20261106120200_progress_awards.sql`: the four triggers, `daily_checkin(_at)`, `set_jarso_look`, `get_progress`, grants.

Then regenerate `packages/db/src/database.types.ts` and update `docs/database.md`, including the lock order. They are pushed to the hosted project only after the user approves.

### pgTAP tests

**`child_facing_name.test.sql`**
- A parent sets their own name.
- A blank or 21-character name is rejected.
- A child cannot update it.
- A child reads their parent's name.

**`progress.test.sql`**
- RLS: two families, siblings, a parent; activity days are hidden from the parent.
- Direct DML is refused with `42501`.
- `set_jarso_look`: a locked look raises `JA409`, an unlocked one works, the constraint is the backstop.
- `progress_level` matches the core constants.

**`progress_awards.test.sql`**
- A purchase gives +10. The 4th log in a day gives 0.
- Undo keeps the Sparks. A fix gives 0.
- A retried purchase key does not award twice.
- Money got gives +10.
- A parent-confirmed deposit gives +20 to the child. An auto-confirmed one does too. The 3rd save in a day gives 0.
- A main step gives +15. A replay step, or the same step after a restart, gives 0.
- Check-in "nope" writes no ledger rows and gives +5 once a day.
- Milestones at 25/50/75/100% and the reached award pay once each.
- A goal created already past a milestone pays 0.
- Lowering the target does not pay.
- A practice-mode row awards nothing.
- A removed child is skipped.
- Badges are granted.

**`progress_streak.test.sql`**
- Seed activity rows as postgres, then:
  - consecutive days;
  - today not yet active;
  - one miss covered;
  - a second miss in the same week breaks the run;
  - two misses in a row break it;
  - Sunday/Monday week edge;
  - restart at 1.
- `daily_checkin_at` across a midnight in a non-UTC family timezone.

### Risks

- **Two awards racing.** Every award locks the `child_progress` row last, after any source, ledger-advisory or attempt lock. Nothing takes the ledger lock after the progress lock, so there is no deadlock. The `spark_events` unique key and the `(child, day)` primary key make retries safe, and a cap read under the row lock is exact.
- **Timezone.** Day boundaries use `now()` at transaction start in `families.timezone`, and DST is handled by `at time zone`. Changing the family timezone can shift a day, which can give one extra or one missing cap window; accept that. `family_today` is security invoker but runs fine inside definer triggers.
- **Drift.** Thresholds, wear levels and badge keys live in both SQL and core. Both test suites pin the same numbers.
- **Triggers on the money path.** Every ledger insert now runs extra code. The `when` clause keeps non-matching rows free, and the exception guard keeps money writes working if an award fails.
- **Reservation changes are not caught.** A cancelled withdrawal raises counted Save without a ledger row, so the milestone pays on the next Save inflow.
- **Practice mode is excluded.** That is my call. Revisit it if practice logs should earn Sparks.

## Findings (Jarso, read-only)

### 1. packages/data module pattern (goals and tour)
- Layout: `packages/data/src/<x>/index.ts` is just `export * from "./repository";`, plus any extra files (goals also has `view.ts`). Tour has only `index.ts` and `repository.ts`.
- Export: each module gets its own subpath in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/package.json` `exports`, e.g. `"./tour": "./src/tour/index.ts"`. A new module needs `"./progress": "./src/progress/index.ts"`. Deps are `@jarso/core`, `@jarso/db` and supabase-js.
- Repository style (`/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/src/goals/repository.ts`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/src/tour/repository.ts`):
  - `type Client = SupabaseClient<Database>` (Database comes from `@jarso/db`). Each function takes `(db: Client, ...)` as its first argument.
  - Reads use `db.from("child_tours").select("buddy, finished_at").eq("child_id", childId).maybeSingle()`. The function maps snake_case to camelCase by hand, wraps bigint money in `Number(...)`, and narrows enums with a type guard (`isCoach`).
  - Errors: `if (error) throw toAppError(error, "Failed to load the tour")`. `toAppError` comes from `@jarso/core/lib` (`packages/core/src/lib/db-error.ts`). It maps known PG codes to an AppError, logs anything else and returns an INTERNAL error with a generic message. For domain errors you can throw `new AppError("VALIDATION", msg, { fieldErrors, cause })` yourself. `callSaveGoal` does this for code `22023`.
  - Naming: `find*` for reads, `call*` for RPC wrappers in goals, and `saveTour` in tour.
- Tests: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/tests/<x>/repository.test.ts`, run by Vitest (`include: tests/**/*.test.ts`). Mocking goes through `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/tests/fake-client.ts`:
  - `fakeClient(tables, rpcs)` returns `{ db, from, rpc, queries }`. Results are `ok(data)` or `{data:null, error:{code,message}}`.
  - Assertions look like `expect(queries[0]?.calls).toContainEqual(["eq","child_id","c1"])` and `expect(rpc).toHaveBeenCalledWith("goal_progress", { p_child_id: "c1" })`.
  - Tests import through the package path (`@jarso/data/goals`).
  - Tour has no data tests, so goals is the better template.

### 2. packages/core module pattern
- Tour (`/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/src/tour/`) has `content.ts` (Zod-parses JSON from `@jarso/content/tour`), `schemas.ts` (Zod input plus `z.infer` types), `steps.ts` (rules plus a `tourCopy` const), `types.ts` and `voice.ts`.
- Goals (`/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/src/goals/`) has `schemas.ts`, `types.ts`, `copy.ts`, `progress.ts`, `predict.ts`, `staircase.ts`, `plan-note.ts` and `emoji.ts`.
- Barrel: `index.ts` with `export * from "./x"` per file and `export type * from "./types"`. Register the subpath in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/package.json` `exports` (for example `"./tour": "./src/tour/index.ts"`).
- Tests go in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/tests/<x>/*.test.ts` (for example `tests/goals/schemas.test.ts`, `tests/tour/steps.test.ts`) and import from `@jarso/core/<x>`.

### 3. Web
- Yes, web uses a re-export pattern:
  - `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/web/src/features/goals/server/repository.ts` is `import "server-only"; export * from "@jarso/data/goals";`.
  - Tour's version re-exports by name: `export { findTour, saveTour } from "@jarso/data/tour";`.
- Around that sit `service.ts` (auth via `assertUser` / `assertSelfOrParentOf`, then `createClient()` from `@/lib/supabase/server`), `actions.ts` (`createAction(schema, fn)`), `server/index.ts` (the server-only barrel) and the feature `index.ts` (re-exports `@jarso/core/<x>` plus components).
- If web has no progress UI, it needs nothing now. The only exception is if some web-side write (an RPC, trigger or server action) should award Sparks. That would belong in the SQL function or trigger anyway, not in web code.

### 4. Mobile TanStack hooks
- `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/mobile/src/features/goals/use-goal.ts`: `const { userId } = useSession()` from `@/features/auth`, then `useQuery({ queryKey: keys.goal(userId ?? ""), enabled: Boolean(userId), queryFn: () => getGoalView(supabase, userId as string) })`. The client comes from `@/lib/supabase/client`.
- `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/mobile/src/features/goals/use-goal-mutations.ts`:
  - A `useRefreshGoal()` helper calls `queryClient.invalidateQueries` on `keys.goal(id)` and `keys.home(id)`.
  - Mutations are `useMutation({ mutationFn: (input) => callSaveGoal(supabase, input), onSuccess: refresh })`, with optional Haptics.
  - `useMarkCelebrated` deliberately skips the refresh.
- Keys live in one place, the `keys` object in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/mobile/src/lib/query.ts`. Child keys look like `goal: (userId) => ["goal", userId] as const`. Sub-keys share a prefix so one invalidation refreshes the whole group (for example `practice: ["learning", userId, "practice"]`). A `progress(userId)` key would be added there.
- The global QueryCache `onError` handles UNAUTHORIZED AppErrors (it signs the child out).

### 5. RPC calls
RPCs are called with `db.rpc(...)` inside data repos. Example from goals:
```ts
const { data, error } = await db.rpc("goal_progress", { p_child_id: childId });
if (error) throw toAppError(error, "We couldn't load your goal.");
const row = data?.[0];
```
Nullable arguments need a cast because the generated types drop nullability: `p_buddy: (input.buddy ?? null) as string`.

### 6. database.types.ts (`/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/db/src/database.types.ts`)
- It is generated by `pnpm db:types` (`supabase gen types typescript --local > packages/db/src/database.types.ts`, defined in the root package.json) and re-exported from `packages/db/src/index.ts`.
- Tables appear under `public.Tables.<name>` with `Row`, `Insert`, `Update` and `Relationships`. `child_tours` is at about line 206.
- Functions appear under `public.Functions`:
  - `"save_welcome_tour": { Args: { p_buddy: string, p_finished: boolean }; Returns: undefined }`
  - A table-returning function gives an array: `goal_progress` returns `{counted_minor, goal_id, reached}[]`.
- Tests depend on it only through types: `fakeClient` is cast to `SupabaseClient<Database>`, so a new table or RPC must be in the regenerated types before the repo and tests typecheck. Nothing about it is checked at runtime.

### 7. Welcome tour (child_tours) end to end
- Migration: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261106100000_welcome_tour.sql` (75 lines).
  - Table keyed by `child_id` (PK), with `family_id`, a check-constrained `buddy`, `finished_at`, timestamps and an `updated_at` trigger.
  - RLS: a SELECT policy using `is_self(child_id) or is_parent_of(child_id)`. Insert, update and delete are revoked from `authenticated`.
  - Writes go only through the `security definer` RPC `save_welcome_tour`. It looks up the approved child in `family_members`, raises `JA403` otherwise and `22023` on bad input.
- pgTAP test: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/welcome_tour.test.sql`
- Data: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/data/src/tour/repository.ts` (`findTour`, `saveTour`). It has no data tests.
- Core: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/src/tour/` (`saveTourSchema`, `TourState`, `tourSteps`, `tourCopy`), with tests in `packages/core/tests/tour/`. Words come from `packages/content/src/tour`.
- Web: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/web/src/features/tour/`:
  - `server/{repository,service,actions,index}.ts`
  - `components/{welcome-tour,replay-tour-button,tour-store,tour-voice}`
  - Used in `apps/web/src/app/(app)/child/layout.tsx` (`getTour`, `welcomeTourOn` behind the `FEATURE_WELCOME_TOUR` env flag) and `child/settings/page.tsx`.
  - Test: `apps/web/tests/features/tour/welcome-tour.test.tsx`
- Mobile: no tour usage at all. There is no `apps/mobile/src/features/tour` and no `keys.tour`, so there is no existing mobile precedent for this table.

### Related existing streak code
Check this before defining a new streak, to avoid a duplicate:
- `packages/core/src/learning/score.ts`
- `packages/core/src/learning/comeback.ts`
- `packages/core/src/learning/types.ts`
- `packages/data/src/learning/overview.ts`
- `packages/core/src/dashboard/learning.ts`
- `packages/core/src/notifications/moments.ts`

- `progress_award` raises JA404 for a non-child and 22023 for bad input.
- Task 4 must set `child_activity_days.checkin` before the check-in award, or the day won't count for the streak.

Task 3 review, fix round 1 (supabase/migrations/20261106120100_progress.sql + its pgTAP files). Fix:

(Important) ~L477-483: the replay check returns 0 before the day upsert, so a repeated key (e.g. same mission step redone on a later day after a new attempt) no longer marks the day active → real learning day breaks the streak. Fix: in the replay branch, before `return 0`, `if p_kind in ('log','step') then perform public.progress_mark_active(p_child_id, p_kind, p_now); end if;`. pgTAP: award `step:m:s` on day 1, same key on day 2 (via p_now) → day 2 active.
2. (Important) Lock-order inversion in the check-in handoff (your report L94 + comment ~L552 tell daily_checkin_at to upsert checkin then call progress_award, i.e. day row before child_progress). Controller ruling: add `p_checkin text default null` as the LAST parameter of `progress_award` (keep existing parameter order; validate in ('yes','nope') when kind='checkin', 22023 otherwise), and write it inside the existing day upsert under the row lock with `checkin = coalesce(d.checkin, excluded.checkin)` (first answer of the day wins). Callers never touch child_activity_days directly. Update the comment and your report's handoff note accordingly. pgTAP: checkin award with p_checkin='nope' sets the day's checkin and makes the day active; a second call keeps the first answer.
3. (Minor→do) Ruling: `goals_reached_count` bumps only when the goal_reached award is a real award (amount > 0 path, i.e. not silenced by p_award=false and not inside the 7-day cooldown). So the goal_reached badge means actually saving. pgTAP: goal created already ≥ target → goals_reached_count stays 0.
4. (Minor) progress_mark_active comment ~L580-591: correct the claim — it can consume one step-cap slot when the day had no step activity; say so plainly (accepted).
5. (Minor) Add cheap pgTAP cases: save cap (3rd save that day → 0), same-day check-in second call → 0, a silenced goal_milestone (0) never pays later. Update plan counts.

### Spec Compliance
Compliant. Findings:

- Exports, `package.json`, `levelFor`, `levelProgress`, `parentLabel`, `wearForLevel`, `progressCopy` and `checkinAnswerSchema` all match the brief. `childFacingNameSchema` trims, enforces 1-20 characters and accepts null or undefined.
- The core progress tests pass: 3 files, 20 tests. I did not run typecheck or Biome.
- Child copy has no "lost", "broke" or "fail".
- Badge `how` text matches the SQL rules in the brief, so none contradict them:
  - `diary_week` ("Write in your diary 7 days running") matches logs on 7 consecutive days.
  - `goal_half` ("Reach half of a goal") matches a goal at 50% or more.
  - The streak badges use `best_streak`, and the count badges use counters that rise only on an awarded event.
- **Icons:** all 23 resolve in `apps/mobile`'s `lucide-react-native` 1.66.0 (kebab to Pascal). One needs care. `book-marked` has no `.mjs` file because the package aliases `BookMarked` to `BookBookmark`. A lookup by exported name works, but a file-based or `dynamicIconImports` lookup would miss it.
- **`progressSchema` shape:** a null `nextLevelAt` parses, and the test covers it.

### Issues
#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None.

#### Minor
- `packages/core/src/progress/schemas.ts:17-20` (`look`): the schema is strict. A `lid` or `wear` value outside the mascot enums (for example one added later on the server) makes the whole `get_progress` parse throw. The mascot module's own `parseLook` uses `.catch(DEFAULT_LOOK.*)` for exactly this case. The DB CHECK constraints make it unlikely today, but the badges array is already tolerant, so `look` should be too. Fix: add `.catch(DEFAULT_LOOK.lid)` and `.catch(DEFAULT_LOOK.wear)`, or use `z.unknown().transform(parseLook)`.
- `packages/core/src/progress/badges.ts` (`BADGES.logs_100`): `icon: "book-marked"` is only an alias in lucide. Consider `book-bookmark` or `book-open-check` so the icon also resolves by file or kebab-case name. This is optional.
- `packages/core/tests/progress`: `wearForLevel` and `levelProgress` boundary cases are covered, but there is no test for a `look` with an unknown enum value. Add one when you fix the `look` item above.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the spec, the constants and badge keys are exact, all icons resolve, and the tests pass. Only minor hardening remains, chiefly making `look` parsing tolerant.

- **Wear check.** The `child_progress_wear_unlocked` check calls an immutable function that is created before the table. That is fine.
- **RLS.** It is on for all four tables, with explicit policies for select, insert, update and delete. `child_activity_days` is readable by the child only. The `(select public.is_self(child_id))` form matches the repo pattern (`20261029182402_money_rules.sql:56`). The revokes from `anon` and `authenticated` match the Global Constraints.
- **Mirrors.**
  - The level thresholds are exact.
  - Wear levels are none 1, cap 1, shades 3, headphones 4, crown 5.
  - Caps are log 3, save 2, step 20, check-in 1 (the `checkin:<day>` key plus the same-day check), and goal reached 7 days by `created_at`.
- **Streak.** I traced every streak fixture by hand and all of them match:
  - 3 days → 3/false
  - today open → 2/false
  - 03,05,06 → 3/true
  - 03,04 with today open → 2/true
  - 02,04,06 → 2/true
  - 01,02,05,06 → 2/false
  - Sunday 04,06,07,09 on day 09 → 3/false
  - 06,07,09,11 → 4/true
  - after a break → 1/false
  - the award case → streak 7, best 7, `last_active_day` 11-06, with `streak_3`, `streak_7` and `diary_week`

  Sunday 11-08 truncates to the week of 11-02, so the ISO-week logic holds.
- **`diary_week`.** It needs exactly 7 days with `logs > 0` in the 7 days ending `p_day`. The primary key stops a day counting twice. Correct.
- **Goal milestones.** They are 25, or 0 when `p_award` is false, and each key is recorded once for life.
- **Backfill.** It only adds approved children and running it again adds nothing.
- **Revokes.** Every internal function is revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`.

Compile-level checks:
- **No name clashes.** `progress_streak` returns columns `streak` and `rest_used_this_week`. The body never uses those names unqualified in SQL, so there is no "ambiguous column" error.
- **Upsert.** `insert … as d … on conflict (child_id, day) do update … returning (case p_kind … d.logs …)` is valid; the smallint and int branches resolve to int.
- **Locking select.** `select cp.* into v_row … for update of cp` with an `exists` subquery is legal.
- **Null composite.** `return null` from a function returning a composite, assigned to a rowtype variable, gives a row whose fields are all null, so the `v_row.child_id is null` guard works.
- **Small constructs.** These are all valid:
  - `date[] || date`
  - `foreach … in array array[…]`
  - `pg_catalog.count(*)`
  - `not between`
  - `get diagnostics`
  - the unnamed `for i in 1..400` loop variable
- **Operators.** pg_catalog operators resolve even with `search_path = ''`.
- **`family_today`.** It runs as security invoker inside the definer functions, so it runs as the owner and reads `families` correctly.

pgTAP:
- **Plan counts.** I counted 55 and 14 assertions, matching the plans.
- **Helpers.** `tests.create_user`, `authenticate_as` and `clear_auth` exist in `_helpers.psql`.
- **Fixtures.** `create_family(text, text) returns uuid` and the 5-argument `register_child` match other tests (`allowance_generation.test.sql:18`).
- **Results.** Every expected value matches my trace:
  - 30, level 1, `logs_count` 3, and 4 logs on the day
  - c2: 110 Sparks, level 2, `goals_reached_count` 2
  - c3 in Asia/Kolkata: day 11-07 is active
  - c1 sees 4 events and 2 activity days
  - parent A sees 2 progress rows and no activity days
  - the 42501 privilege errors and the anon 42501

Deviations, judged:
- **D1 (inline the goal_progress formula):** justified. `goal_progress` raises JA403 under cron and pgTAP and writes `reached_at`. The formula matches `balance('save','recorded') − reserved_save`, and neither function is redefined later.
- **D2 (extra internal functions `progress_refresh_streak` and `progress_backfill`):** fine.
- **D3 (replay check before the day counter):** sound for cap exactness, but it lost the design's rule that the activity "always counts toward the streak". See Important #1.
- **D4 (over-cap doesn't bump the `*_count` columns):** consistent with design step 6.
- **D5 (0-amount `goal_reached` event inside the 7-day window):** a sensible fill for an ambiguity.
- **D6 (check-in answer set by the caller):** the handoff contract it creates inverts the lock order. See Important #2.
- **D7 (input validation, JA404 and 22023):** good.
- **D8 (`authenticated` may call the pure level and wear functions):** fine.

### Issues

#### Critical
None found.

#### Important

1. **`supabase/migrations/20261106120100_progress.sql:477-483`: a repeated award key no longer marks the day active.**
   - **What.** The replay check returns 0 before the day upsert, so the day's activity row is never touched.
   - **Why it matters.** Step keys are `step:<mission_key>:<step_key>`. A child can start a new attempt of the same mission after an abandoned or completed one: `mission_attempts_one_open_idx` only blocks two open attempts, and `step_results` is keyed by `attempt_id`. That child's main-phase steps on a later day hit an existing key. They get 0 Sparks, which is correct, but the day is never marked active, so a real day of learning breaks the streak. The design's order (bump the counter, then `on conflict`) did count it, and the Global Constraints say a step makes a day active.
   - **Fix.** Inside the replay branch, before `return 0`, add:
     ```sql
     if p_kind in ('log','step') then perform public.progress_mark_active(p_child_id, p_kind, p_now); end if;
     ```
     `progress_mark_active` re-locks the row this transaction already holds, which is safe, and `greatest(x,1)` never adds a cap slot when the day already has activity.
   - **Test.** Add a pgTAP case: award `step:m:s` on day 1, award the same key on day 2, then assert that day 2 is active.

2. **The handoff contract in `task-3-report.md:94` and the function comment at `20261106120100_progress.sql:552` invert the lock order.**
   - **What.** The contract tells `daily_checkin_at` (Task 4) to upsert `checkin` on the day row and then call `progress_award`. That takes the `child_activity_days` row lock first and the `child_progress` lock second. `progress_award` takes them in the other order: `progress_lock` first (line ~472), then the day upsert.
   - **Why it matters.** A check-in racing a purchase or step trigger for the same child on the same day can deadlock. Postgres then aborts one of them. If the trigger loses, its `exception when others` guard swallows the error and the award is silently lost. This breaks the Global Constraints rule that `child_progress` is locked last, relative to Sparks state.
   - **Fix, preferred.** Let `progress_award` set the answer itself. Add an optional `p_checkin text default null` and write `checkin` in the same upsert under the row lock:
     ```sql
     checkin = coalesce(d.checkin, excluded.checkin)
     ```
     The first answer of the day wins.
   - **Fix, minimum.** Change the contract and the comment so `daily_checkin_at` calls `public.progress_lock(child)` before it upserts `checkin`.

#### Minor

3. **`20261106120100_progress.sql:580-591`: `progress_mark_active` can use up a cap slot.**
   - **What.** The comment says it "never uses up a cap slot twice", but it can use one. If the day has no step activity when `mark_active` runs (for example, an attempt that crosses midnight runs its replay phase first on the new day), steps go 0→1, and the first paid step then sees `v_count = 2`. That leaves 19 paid steps that day, not 20.
   - **Fix.** Accept it and fix the comment. Or keep replay activity in a separate column, for example `touched boolean` in the `active` expression, so it doesn't share the cap counter.

4. **`20261106120100_progress.sql:651-655` with the `p_award = false` path: a goal can count as reached without any saving.**
   - **What.** A goal that is created already at or above its target, or whose target is lowered, records `goal_reached` with 0 Sparks. It still bumps `goals_reached_count`, which grants the `goal_reached` badge.
   - **Why it matters.** A child can earn that badge by creating a goal with a tiny target. Only one badge is affected, and no Sparks.
   - **Fix.** Confirm with product. If it should not count, only bump `goals_reached_count` when `p_award` is true. That needs a parameter to `progress_award`, or a check of `v_amount > 0 or <not silenced by p_award>`.

5. **`20261106120100_progress.sql:18,32`: `security definer` on the two pure functions is unnecessary.** `progress_level` and `progress_wear_level` touch no tables, and `security definer` stops Postgres inlining them in the check constraint. Harmless, and consistent with the "all functions are security definer" rule, so this is optional.

6. **`supabase/tests/database/progress.test.sql`: some caps are untested.** It covers the log cap only. Add small cases for the save cap (2), the step cap (20, or at least `v_count > cap` for `save`), the same-day check-in returning 0, and `goal_milestone` with amount 0 not paying later. These are cheap now and protect Task 4.

7. **`20261106120100_progress.sql:313`: `plpgsql_check` may warn that the loop variable `i` is unused.** It is a warning only, so it does not fail `--fail-on error`. Ignore it, or use `while v_guard < 400`.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The SQL looks like it compiles, the pgTAP plans and expected values trace correctly, and RLS and the revokes are sound. However, D3 drops activity for repeated award keys, so a child who redoes a mission on a later day loses streak credit. And the check-in handoff contract creates a lock-order inversion that can deadlock against the award triggers. Both are small, local fixes in this migration and its contract.

- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/progress.test.sql
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/progress_streak.test.sql

### Spec Compliance
Meets the brief. All four functions have the specified signatures and behaviour, the `./progress` export is added, `findParentLabels` sits in family/repository.ts next to `listChildMembers`, and the tests cover every listed case. I did not run the tests or typecheck, and I did not write task-5-report.md because I was told read-only; this report is the deliverable.

- `getProgress` calls `rpc("get_progress", { p_child_id })`, throws `toAppError` on error, and parses with `progressSchema`. A malformed result throws `AppError("INTERNAL")`.
- `dailyCheckin` sends `{ p_answer }` and validates the returned shape by hand.
- `setJarsoLook` maps `JA409` to `AppError("VALIDATION", "Not unlocked yet")`. This overrides the db-error table, which maps JA409 to CONFLICT, and that is correct per the brief.
- `findParentLabels` filters on approved parents in the child's family, orders by `created_at` ascending, and drops nulls.
- `database.types.ts` has the four tables, `profiles.child_facing_name`, `daily_checkin`, `daily_checkin_at`, `get_progress`, `set_jarso_look`, and all the internal `progress_*` functions. `progress_award`, `progress_mark_active`, `progress_check_goal` and `progress_backfill` signatures match the migration. `progress_award` and `daily_checkin_at` args follow the design reference and the migration's `p_now default now()`.

Named risks:
1. **Type fidelity: OK.**
   - Nullability is correct: `checkin`, `last_active_day` and `rest_week` are nullable, the rest are not.
   - Everything with a default is optional in Insert and Update: sparks, level, counters, lid, wear, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `earned_at`, spark_events `id`, and the activity counters.
   - Required in Insert: `child_id`, `family_id`, `day`, `amount`, `kind`, `source_key`, `badge_key`.
   - bigint, smallint and int all map to `number`.
   - The generated `active` column is rendered as Row `boolean | null` with Insert and Update `?: never`. That matches how supabase gen types renders generated columns. There is no other generated column in this file to compare against.
   - `child_progress_child_id_fkey` has `isOneToOne: true`, which is correct because the PK is `child_id`. The other foreign keys are `false`.
   - Keys are alphabetical, as the generator does it.
   - `progress_lock` returns a composite row type and `progress_streak` returns a table. Both look plausible for the generator.
2. **`findParentLabels` under RLS: OK for a child session.**
   - `family_members` select policy ("Family members are viewable by approved members") uses `is_approved_member(family_id)`, so an approved child can read parent rows in their own family.
   - `profiles` select policy ("Profiles are viewable by self and family", family_core.sql:292) lets a user read any approved member of `current_family_id()`. Parent profiles are therefore readable by the child.

### Spec Compliance
The migration matches the brief, the Design Reference and both ledger rulings.

**Rulings:**
- `daily_checkin_at` calls only `progress_award(..., p_now, p_checkin => p_answer)` and never touches `child_activity_days` or the ledger.
- The goal triggers rely on the engine's `goals_reached_count`, which only counts paid goals. The C5 test expects `200:1`, and C6 does not assert a count.

**Schema names, checked against the real migrations:**
- `ledger_entries`: `source_type` (not `source`), `source_id` (uuid, so `coalesce(new.source_id, new.id)` is well-typed), `corrects_entry_id`, `mode`, `kind`, `jar`.
- `gift_in` is written only by `record_money_received_at` with source `diary`. Parent money is `parent_gift_in` or `allowance_in`, so it never earns a log award.
- `deposit_in` is written only by `post_request`, with `source_id` = the request id. Both `complete_request` and the auto-confirm path in `create_deposit` go through it.
- A reversal has kind `reversal`, so it never gets a log award. A fix's new line has `corrects_entry_id` set, so it is skipped.
- Practice rows are excluded by the WHEN clause.
- `step_results.phase` is `'main'` or `'replay'`, and `mission_key` comes from `mission_attempts`.
- `complete_mission` does `update ... set status='completed'`. The trigger's WHEN uses `old.status is distinct from 'completed'`, which is valid on an update trigger.
- `save_goal`'s upsert sets `target_minor`, so `after update of target_minor` fires on the conflict path and AFTER INSERT does not.

**Compile and runtime checks:**
- All four trigger functions `returns trigger`, `return null`, run `for each row`, and are security definer with `search_path=''` and fully qualified names.
- No WHEN clause uses OLD on an insert trigger.
- Every `raise warning` has as many placeholders as arguments (3/3, or 4/4 for the step trigger).
- Exception blocks nested inside `if` branches are valid PL/pgSQL.
- Mixing positional and named arguments is legal.
- `(array[...])[v_level::int]` returns null at level 10.
- `jsonb_agg(... order by ...)` over the `limit 10` subquery is valid and wrapped in `coalesce(..., '[]')`.
- `to_char(date, text)` resolves through the implicit cast to timestamptz.
- The grant and revoke signatures are exact.

**pgTAP file:**
- The plan is 95, and I counted 95 assertions.
- Every fixture call matches the current signature: `record_purchase` (6 args with defaults), `correct_diary_entry` (5), `undo_diary_entry` (returns text), `record_money_received` (child, amount, jar, category, label, key), `create_deposit` (returns `request_id`), `complete_request` (returns `'completed'`), `set_jar_moves_approval`, `start_mission`, `record_step_result` (8 args, returns boolean), `complete_mission(uuid)` (the second argument defaults), and `save_goal` (returns uuid).
- I traced every expected number by hand and all match the caps and values:
  - C1: 10/30/30, `30:3`.
  - C2: 10, then 10 after the fix, `logs=1`, then 20.
  - C3: 20, 40, `40:2`.
  - C4: 15, `15:1`.
  - C5: 25/50/200, `200:1`, 4 milestones; the badges in sort order are goal_half, goal_reached, goal_set.
  - C6: two events at 0 sorted `:25` < `:50`, then 25, then 25, then 2.
  - C7: `{recorded,5}` then `{already,0}`; 18:00Z and 19:00Z land on 2030-01-10 and 2030-01-11 in IST.
  - get_progress for C1 matches exactly: streak and bestStreak 1, nextLevelAt 100, and the keys sort correctly under `collate "C"`.
- The guard test is meaningful. A purchase row for the parent makes `progress_lock` return null, so `progress_award` raises JA404. Without the exception block the insert would fail.

**Blast radius:**
- Every trigger body is fully inside `begin … exception when others` blocks. In the ledger trigger, the key is built inside the block, so even a null key cannot escape.
- No existing suite reads `child_progress`, `spark_events`, `child_badges` or `child_activity_days`, or introspects `pg_trigger`.
- `holds_ledger_lock` assertions (purchases, handover) are unaffected, because the triggers take no advisory lock.
- In `goals.test.sql`, the direct-insert test expects `23505`, and the unique index raises that before any AFTER trigger runs.
- `progress.test.sql:211-216` (the Task 3 suite) now gets the goal-insert trigger before its explicit `progress_check_goal(c3,false)` call. The trigger writes the same 5 zero-amount events, so the explicit call becomes a no-op and its assertions (`(0,10)`, 5, 10, badges) still hold.
- The lock order holds on every path: source lock first (request row, ledger advisory lock, attempt row, goal row), then `child_progress`. `daily_checkin_at` and `set_jarso_look` take only `child_progress`.

**Security:**
- `get_progress` requires `is_self` or `is_parent_of`, and also an approved child membership; otherwise it raises JA403. A sibling, another family's parent and anon are all refused, and the tests cover each.
- `daily_checkin` and `set_jarso_look` are child-only (JA403).
- To parents, `get_progress` exposes only the design's fields (streak, restUsedThisWeek, checkedInToday), never the check-in answer or the per-day rows.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None.

#### Minor
1. **`supabase/migrations/20261106120200_progress_awards.sql:116-118`: repeat completions are counted.** `missions_count` goes up on every completed attempt, including the same mission done again (start_mission allows 40 a day). The `missions_5` and `missions_10` badges can therefore be earned by repeating one mission. The design does say "bumps missions_count", so this is a product call. If the badge should mean distinct missions, fix: count only when no earlier completed attempt exists for the same mission, i.e. `if not exists (select 1 from public.mission_attempts a where a.child_id = new.child_id and a.mission_key = new.mission_key and a.status = 'completed' and a.id <> new.id) then … increment …; end if`. Otherwise leave it as is.
2. **Goal-milestone Sparks can be farmed with self-reported money.** The cause is the ledger trigger's goal branch (lines 40-47) combined with the engine.
   - Steps: the child records money received into Save (+25 for each milestone crossed), undoes it within 24h (Sparks are never taken back), archives the goal and creates a new one, then repeats.
   - Gain: about 100 Sparks per goal. Only the goal-reached bonus has the 7-day cap.
   - The design accepts this (Sparks are not money), so no change is needed. Record it as a known gap in the report or in `docs/domain.md`.
3. **`supabase/tests/database/progress_awards.test.sql:131-135`: one Review Focus case is not covered.** Review Focus item 2 says "a purchase is undone and logged again; the slot isn't freed". The test only undoes a fixed line and never records a new purchase afterwards. Fix: after the undo, record purchases until the 4th of the day for that child and assert it pays 0 (or check `child_activity_days.logs` after the undo).
4. **`supabase/migrations/20261106120200_progress_awards.sql:357-361`: `service_role` keeps EXECUTE.** The trigger functions and `daily_checkin_at` keep Supabase's default EXECUTE grant for `service_role`. This is harmless: trigger functions can't be called directly, and `daily_checkin_at` with no `auth.uid()` raises JA403. It matches the engine and most migrations, though `learning_child_family` and the guide functions also revoke from `service_role`. Optionally add `, service_role` to the internal revokes for consistency.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Every name, signature, WHEN clause and expected test value checks out against the real schema and the reviewed engine. Every trigger body is fully guarded, and no existing suite, including the Task 3 progress suite, should break. The remaining points are product-level or test-coverage polish.

- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/progress_awards.test.sql
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261106120100_progress.sql (engine, for interaction)

### Spec Compliance
Compliant. Every item in the brief is present.
- **Hooks:** `useProgress`, `useDailyCheckin`, `useSetJarsoLook` and `useParentLabel` all exist with the specified behaviour.
  - `useProgress` is enabled only when there is a `userId`.
  - `useParentLabel` takes the first label through `parentLabel(...)`, falls back to "your parent", and has a 24 h stale time.
- **Key:** `keys.progress` is `["progress", userId]`. The extra `keys.parentLabel` is a harmless addition.
- **Invalidation:** `keys.progress(id)` is added after purchase, received, undo, fix, request mutations (deposit, withdrawal, emergency), goal save/archive, mission `finished()`, and the outbox purchase-sent replay.
- **Tests:** there are 6 tests, plus the one diary assertion.
- **Verification:** I did not re-run anything. The report claims 634 tests passing, with typecheck and biome clean.

Named risks:
- **Empty cache:** safe. `onMutate` guards on `if (previous)`, so nothing is written and nothing crashes. On error there is no rollback, and `onSettled` refetches.
- **Rollback:** restores exactly the snapshot taken in `onMutate`. The test is meaningful: no observer is mounted, so the invalidate does not refetch, and the pre-error cache value was `NEW_LOOK`.
- **userId key consistency:** consistent. Every site uses `keys.progress(<child userId>)`. `useSetJarsoLook` uses `?? ""` only before a session exists. In `mission-player`, the `queryKeys` alias is needed because `keys` is shadowed by a local variable.
- **Mission refresh only at finish:**
  - The DB trigger `progress_on_step_result` awards 15 Sparks per step, so Sparks do change per step.
  - The mission player is a self-contained flow, though. Home and Me are not visible mid-mission, and `finished()` refreshes before the child returns to them. The per-step gap has little user impact and does not need fixing for this spec.
- **Offline mission replay:**
  - **Online path:** it works. `finished()` fires after `saveFinish`, which returns once the item is queued.
  - **Offline path:** if the finish is queued and replayed later by the outbox, nothing invalidates `progress` or `learning`. Progress goes stale until the next refetch. This mirrors the existing `learning` gap.
- **Tests assert behaviour:** mostly. They check real cache contents and invalidation calls rather than only mock calls. The coverage gaps are listed below.

### Issues
#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None.

#### Minor
- `apps/mobile/src/lib/outbox/sync.tsx:95-99`: the outbox sync only listens for `onPurchaseSent`. A queued `completeMission` or step-result replay never refreshes `progress` or `learning`, so Sparks, badges and level lag after an offline lesson. Fix: add the same invalidation to the outbox success path for lesson kinds, or accept the gap and note it in `docs/mobile.md`.
- `apps/mobile/tests/features/progress/use-progress.test.tsx`: there is no test for the empty-cache optimistic case, which is the main guard in `useSetJarsoLook`. It also does not assert that `onSettled` invalidates. Fix: add a case with no seeded cache that expects no throw and a call to `invalidateQueries`.
- `apps/mobile/src/features/progress/use-progress.ts:34-37`: with two overlapping look changes, a rollback of the second restores the first optimistic value. This is cosmetic, and `onSettled` refetches to correct it.
- Only the purchase invalidation is tested. Received, undo, fix, requests, goals and the mission player are untested, and the brief allowed that.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The hook interfaces match the brief. Optimistic update, rollback and invalidation are correct, and the key is used consistently at every call site. The only real gap is offline-replayed lesson finishes not refreshing progress, which is minor and mirrors the existing `learning` behaviour.

## Final review: Plan 2, progress backend (uncommitted snapshot 24f274c1..5a25c62b)

### Strengths
- **Core and SQL constants match exactly.** I checked them by hand:
  - Level thresholds `[0..3200]` appear in `progress_level`, and the `nextLevelAt` array is `thresholds[1..9]`, which gives null at level 10 because the index falls out of bounds.
  - Wear levels (none/cap 1, shades 3, headphones 4, crown 5) match `WEAR_UNLOCK_LEVEL`.
  - The five lids match `LID_COLORS`.
  - Values: log 10, save 20, step 15, check-in 5, milestone 25, reached 100.
  - Caps: log 3, save 2, step 20, check-in 1, cooldown 7 days.
  - The 23 badge keys are identical, in the same order, in `badges.ts` and `progress_check_badges`.
- **`get_progress` JSON matches `progressSchema`.** The ten keys are camelCase. Nulls are coalesced for a child with no row yet. `nextLevelAt` is nullable. Timestamptz values become ISO strings, and unknown badge keys are filtered in the transform.
- **Error mapping lines up.** JA403 maps to FORBIDDEN, 22023 to VALIDATION, and JA409 is overridden to VALIDATION "Not unlocked yet". Without that override the generic map would say "That already exists". JA404 can only come from internal functions and never reaches a client RPC.
- **Lock order holds.**
  - `save_goal` takes the goal row and then the progress row.
  - `post_request` takes the request, then the advisory lock, then the progress row.
  - No progress function locks a goal, request or attempt row, so there is no inversion.
  - The p_checkin change removed the check-in inversion.
- **Grants, RLS and data hygiene are right.**
  - Trigger guards are per-award savepoints.
  - Child-only activity days keep check-in answers away from parents (rule 15).
  - `profile_id unique` on `family_members` means a stale `child_progress.family_id` cannot happen.
  - Data and core stay pure (rule 18).
- **Mobile hooks follow the plan.** The optimistic look change has a rollback, and the 24 h parent-label query works as specified.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important
1. **`packages/db/src/database.types.ts:1499`: the `progress_award` Args lack `p_checkin?: string`.**
   - Why: the final migration (`20261106120100_progress.sql:441-448`) has a sixth parameter. Rule 7 says the types must match the migrations, and Task 8's `db:types:linked` diff will not be empty.
   - Status: already queued.
   - Fix: add `"p_checkin"?: string` in alphabetical position.
2. **`20261106120200_progress_awards.sql:116-118`: `missions_count` adds 1 for every completed attempt.**
   - Why: a replayed mission (a new attempt for the same `mission_key`) inflates `first_mission`, `missions_5` and `missions_10`, which goes against the ruling that the count is of distinct missions.
   - Status: queued.
   - Fix: only bump when no earlier completed attempt for `(child_id, mission_key)` exists other than `new.id`. Update the wording "counts the mission" in docs/database.md:222 to match.
3. **`apps/mobile/src/features/news/notice-listener.tsx:21-28`: the child's `affected` keys refresh home, history, requests and goal but not `keys.progress(userId)`.**
   - Why: the largest awards come from actions the parent takes on another device:
     - a deposit confirmed through `post_request`, worth +20;
     - goal milestones of +25 each and a goal reached at +100;
     - an allowance or gift landing in Save, which can cross milestones.
   - These can also cause level-ups and badges. The child gets the notice and sees the new balance, but Sparks, level and badges stay stale.
   - The app has no `focusManager`/AppState wiring, so React Query never refetches when the app comes back to the foreground. Plan 3 puts Sparks on Home, which is always mounted.
   - The plan only listed the put-in mutation, but a child would expect the Sparks to move with the notice.
   - Fix: add `keys.progress(userId)` to the child list (one line) and update the listener test if it asserts the list.
4. **`docs/domain.md:90` says "a child cannot farm them by repeating an action".**
   - Why: the ruling accepted a gap. Goal milestones can be farmed with self-reported money into Save, then undo, then a new goal. The domain doc currently claims the opposite.
   - Status: the "known gap" text is queued and not yet written.
   - Fix: add the gap sentence and soften the claim.

#### Minor
5. **`docs/database.md:65` and the header of `20261106120000_child_facing_name.sql:1` say "null = use `full_name`".**
   - Why: the code drops nulls and falls back to "your parent" (`findParentLabels`, `parentLabel`). That is the better choice for minimum data, so the doc and the SQL comment are wrong, not the code.
   - Fix: change both to "null = 'your parent'". Fix before merge (a one-line doc change).
6. **`packages/core/src/progress/schemas.ts:17-20`: `look` uses strict enums (deferred minor from T1).**
   - Why: if the server ever adds a lid colour or outfit, older phone builds fail the whole `getProgress` parse with INTERNAL. That is the same forward-compatibility problem the badge filter was built to avoid.
   - Fix: `look: z.unknown().transform(parseLook)`, plus the missing "unknown look" test. Fix before merge (cheap).
7. **`20261106120100_progress.sql:419-425`: `goal_half` is granted from a milestone that paid 0.**
   - Example: a goal created when savings already cover more than half of it.
   - This is inconsistent with the ruling that `goal_reached` must mean saving. It is defensible because the child did save the money. Later, or add `and se.amount > 0` if the ruling should apply to both badges.
8. **`apps/mobile/src/features/learning/mission-player.tsx:181`: progress is invalidated only at `finished()`, not after each step as Task 6 worded it.** That is fine while no UI shows during a mission. The offline/outbox replay gap is already queued. Later.
9. **`apps/mobile/src/features/home/home-screen.tsx:51`: pull-to-refresh does not include `keys.progress`.** Add it in Plan 3 when Home renders Sparks. Later.
10. **`child_activity_days.goal_awards` is counted but never read or capped.** A daily cap on `goal_milestone`, such as 4 a day, would close most of the accepted farming gap at almost no cost. This is optional and the ruling stands. Later.

### Deferred-minor triage

| Item | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| T2: no test that a parent cannot write other profile columns | Later. The column grant list enforces it. |
| T1: `progressSchema` look strict | **Fix before merge** (#6). |
| T1: `logs_100` icon `book-marked` is an alias | Later. It still renders. |
| T1: no unknown-look test | **Fix before merge**, together with #6. |
| T5: misplaced comment in `packages/data/src/progress/repository.ts:5` (it sits between imports) | Fix in the wave (cosmetic, zero cost). |
| T5: re-diff the types after `db:types:linked` | Task 8 gate, already planned. |
| T3: `security definer` on the pure level/wear functions blocks inlining | Later. Negligible at about 60 users. |
| T3: unused loop variable `i` (plpgsql_check warning) | Later. CI runs `db lint --fail-on error`, so a warning does not fail it. |
| T4: pgTAP case for undo, then log again, with the slot not freed | **Fix before merge.** It is queued and is Review Focus 2; it is not in the test file yet. |
| T4: service_role keeps EXECUTE on internal functions | Later. service_role is trusted. |
| T6: outbox lesson replay does not invalidate progress | Queued. It can ship with #3 or slip to Plan 3 (later is acceptable). |
| T6: no optimistic test with an empty cache | Fix in the wave (cheap), not blocking. |
| T6: rollback when look changes overlap | Later (cosmetic). |

### Declined to judge
- Goal reached paying 0 forever when it falls inside the 7-day cooldown: the design's binding ruling.
- Goal-milestone farming through self-reported Save money: accepted by ruling; I only checked that it gets documented.
- Over-cap steps leaving no event, so they can pay on a later attempt: matches "once for life" read as "paid once"; the spec is silent.
- Emergency requests not counted in the `reserved_save` mirror: it mirrors the current `reserved_save` exactly, and that function's scope is outside this plan.
- Whether SQL behaviour (row counts, `plan(95)`) holds at runtime: SQL has never run; the per-task reviews traced it and pgTAP runs in CI.
- Parent-side progress UI and the parent's `["child"]` keys: Plans 3–8.
- Refetch on foreground through `focusManager`: an existing app-wide gap, not introduced here (it only raises the weight of #3).
- `child_facing_name` grant mechanics beyond the doc: the Task 2 review already covered them.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge?** With fixes.

**Reasoning:** The seams between core, SQL, types, the data layer and mobile are consistent, and there are no correctness or security defects in the award engine. Before merge, apply the queued wave (p_checkin types, distinct missions, the domain gap note, the undo-relog pgTAP case), add `keys.progress` to the child notice listener, make the `look` parse lenient, and fix the "null = full_name" doc lines. Then the user-gated push and the CI pgTAP run.

Key files: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/db/src/database.types.ts`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261106120200_progress_awards.sql`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/apps/mobile/src/features/news/notice-listener.tsx`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/packages/core/src/progress/schemas.ts`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/domain.md`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/database.md`

timestamp: 2026-11-06T05:01:52.795Z

Fixed. Committed on local branch `fix/ci-e2e-supabase-port`, commit 13da947c `fix(ci): reserve supabase ports from ephemeral range`. Not pushed, no PR opened.

**Root cause:** port 54322 sits inside the Linux ephemeral port range (32768-60999). The kernel can give it to an outbound connection as its source port, and then Docker can't bind 0.0.0.0:54322. I can't prove this from the logs, but every other explanation is ruled out:
- **Not a shared runner.** e2e (2) ran on a GitHub-hosted runner (ubuntu-latest, image ubuntu-26.04 20261101.186.1, Azure canadaeast). Each shard gets its own VM, so the shards can't collide.
- **Nothing in the workflow holds the port.** The job has no `services:` block, no earlier `supabase start` or Postgres step, and nothing in it binds 54320-54329.
- **Timing fits.** The failure came about 4s after "Starting database...", right after `supabase start` pulled images in parallel, so many outbound connections were open at that moment.
- **First occurrence.** This is the only port-bind failure in the last 40 CI runs. The other two failures in that window (42103382761, 42097805644) were unrelated test failures.
- **Not runner Postgres.** If the image's own Postgres were running it would sit on 5432, not 54322.

**Fix** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` only): a new first step, "Reserve the local Supabase ports", runs `sudo sysctl -w net.ipv4.ip_local_reserved_ports=54320-54329`. That range covers every port in `supabase/config.toml`, and the kernel will no longer hand those ports out as source ports. It's in the e2e job with a short explanation comment. I also added it to the migrations job, which runs `supabase db start` and has the same exposure. No retry and no `supabase stop` cleanup, because there was no leftover container to clean up.

**Validated:**
- `yaml.safe_load` and actionlint both pass.
- The pre-commit hook passed (Biome and typecheck). I had to run `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline` in the worktree first, because the hook failed with biome not found.

**Uncertain:**
- It's a one-off, so the root cause is inferred rather than captured. The sysctl only covers ports the kernel picks automatically, not something that binds 54322 on purpose, but there's no sign of the latter.
- Re-running main, or the next two-shard push, will confirm it.

Root cause found and reproduced. It was not email, not rate limits and not a slow server. It was hypothesis 3 in a different form: the sign-up form was swapped out after the test had typed into it.

**Root cause.** `/signup` and `/login` wrap the form in `<Suspense fallback={<SignUpForm />}>` (the real form reads `searchParams`). React 19.3 swaps in the streamed real form about 100 ms after the `load` event. Playwright had already typed into the placeholder form (its labels' ids come first in the DOM). After the swap the real, empty form was submitted, Zod returned field errors such as "Name is required", and the page stayed on `/signup`. Real users who type during that window lose their input too, so this was an app bug, not just a test problem.

**Evidence:**
- **Frequency:** sign-up timeouts in 23 of 43 e2e jobs across the last 40 CI runs, and in most full-suite jobs. Retries usually pass.
- **Server is fast:** none of the 43 job logs has a "Slow upstream call" warning (every Supabase call took under 1 s), a "Sign-up rejected" warning, or a "failed to get redirect response" error. The `otp_expired` line comes from email-confirm.spec reusing a link on purpose. The `refresh_token_not_found` lines come from the specs that expire tokens. Neither is related.
- **Why the message said "nothing":** every failure is "Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded". The helper waits 45 s, so the test timeout closes the page before the diagnostic can read it. CI also uploads `apps/web/playwright-report`, which the `github` reporter never creates, so no trace or error-context exists for any run.
- **Reproduction (no Docker):** I ran a production build against a small stub Supabase in /tmp with 4 parallel sign-ups under CPU load. Before the fix, 7 of 80 sign-ups failed with "Name is required…". A DOM log showed the form subtree removed after load. After the fix, 0 of 80 failed, with a slowest click-to-setup time of 1.1 s.

**Fix** (branch `fix/e2e-signup-flake`, not pushed):
- `87f8049b` fix(auth): keep typing out of the placeholder forms. A `placeholder` prop on `SignUpForm` and `SignInForm` disables their inputs and button. `signup/page.tsx` and `login/page.tsx` pass it to the Suspense fallback, so Playwright waits for the real form and users can't type into one that is about to be replaced. New unit test: `apps/web/tests/features/auth/components/auth-forms.test.tsx`.
- `d4b15c26` test(e2e): end the sign-up wait before the test timeout. `expectSetupPage` in `apps/web/tests/e2e/helpers.ts` now waits 20 s instead of 45 s, so a future failure reports what the page showed.

**Validation:** `pnpm check` passes (Biome, typecheck, unit tests), the production build passes, and the stub-based stress run above passed. I didn't run the `/verify` skill because its e2e and database steps need Docker. CI will run the real e2e.

**Residual:**
- The stub stood in for GoTrue and PostgREST, so CI is the real confirmation.
- `SLOW_AUTH_MS = 45_000` and its comment in the two forms rest on the old wrong diagnosis ("server slow under load"). I left them alone.
- Recommended, not done: change the CI upload path to `apps/web/test-results`, but check first that traces holding local session cookies are acceptable in a public repo.
